# REVIEW-058 round three — the fixes to round two, and the last round

You are an adversarial reviewer. This is the third and **last** round of a review
of a proposed design for a public, read-only demo page and of shipped code it
reuses. Round two found three fixes from round one that had not discharged their
findings, all by sweeping the whole tree for the sentence a fix corrected. The
author has fixed what round two upheld. **Your subject is those fixes.** The
rotation closes after this round, whatever it finds: anything you find will be
fixed and recorded, and nobody will review those fixes. So be precise, and say
for each finding exactly what would discharge it.

## STEP 0 — do this before anything else, and report the results first

1. Run `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://github.com` and
   `curl -s -o /dev/null -w '%{http_code}\n' https://s.devnet.rippletest.net:51234/`.
   Print both codes. If network fails, print `NO NETWORK` and continue without it.
2. Run `pwd` and `git log --oneline -1`. The working copy is a clone at commit
   `29fc4ebacd24918e245e38f678dc3f621c054a69`. Work only inside this clone.
3. **Do not read** any directory outside this clone, and in particular: any path
   under `~/.claude`, `~/.codex`, `~/.wconf`, `/tmp/wconf`, any `scratchpad`
   directory, and the repository this clone was made from. Those hold real
   (Devnet, no-value) secrets. **Never print, cat or copy them.** Do not run
   `cargo`, `make check-zeroize-bounds`, `tools/rebuild-env.sh`, or
   `make check-no-secrets` without `--no-local-store`. Do not print seed or key
   literals from source files; refer to them by file and line.
4. You may write scratch files under `$TMPDIR`, run `bun install --frozen-lockfile`
   and `bun test`, and edit files in this clone to break them, as long as you
   restore them. Do not commit, push, or create, rename or edit anything on GitHub
   or anywhere else. Read-only network calls only, Devnet only.
5. ⚠ **Your transcript is committed to this repository.** Do not run `git log -p`,
   `git show`, `git blame` or `git diff` on `UPSTREAM-FEEDBACK.md` at any commit
   other than `29fc4eb`.
6. At the end, list every file you opened.

## What you are reviewing

- **The fixes:** `git diff 629f216 29fc4eb`. Read the changed files **whole**.
- **The ruling the fixes answer:**
  `docs/reviews/REVIEW-058d-adjudication-round-two-2026-10-19.md`. Its table lists

## The three seams. Answer every one, each with an explicit verdict

List findings as `R3-n | HIGH/MEDIUM/LOW | claim | evidence you ran or read, with
paths and line numbers | exactly what would discharge it`. **If a seam has no
finding, write `Seam N: no findings` and say what you tried.** Grade HIGH only for
something that would publish a false statement of fact, expose a secret or private
datum, give the hosted page a signing or decryption path, or leave a round-two
finding undischarged.

### Seam 1 — is every upheld round-two finding discharged, at every site?

Go down `REVIEW-058d`'s table row by row and check each fix against what the
finding **said** in the raw reports, not against the adjudication's summary. Then

### Seam 2 — the two code fixes, broken

- `facility/src/loopback.ts`: one origin predicate now serves positive and negative

  `validated` is absent, and anything else you think a node could send. Confirm
  against the public Devnet endpoint, read-only, that no honest pinned answer the
  facility or web tier uses is now refused.
- `tools/check-disclosure-phrases.ts`: the scan now flattens whitespace across
  lines. Find a wrapped copy it still misses, a true sentence it now rejects, and
  any change in what it reports for the existing tree. Say whether the gate's

### Seam 3 — new sentences

Revision 3 of `ADR-0004`, architecture §6 step 9, `REVIEW-058d`, `CLAIMS.md` E-327
and E-329, `web/api/capability.ts`'s browser-wallet row, and the gate's comments
all changed. Find any sentence that is false, overstated, or a count that does not
match what it counts.

    75	**R2-4 | MEDIUM | TypeScript does not perform Rust’s full ledger-origin checks |** `facility/src/loopback.ts:239–253,591` accepts numeric coercion and `ledger_current_index`, without requiring `validated: true`. I supplied negative answers with matching `ledger_index` and `validated:false`, with only `ledger_current_index`, and with string `"42"`; all became `null`. Rust’s `signer/src/ledger.rs:702–718` requires `validated:true` and a strict `ledger_index`, including on tolerated negatives through `:933–943`. Rust also tolerates only `entryNotFound`, whereas TypeScript includes `objectNotFound`. **Smallest fix:** share the same strict origin predicate across positive and tolerated-negative TypeScript answers, or explicitly document the weaker contract.

    93	
    94	**R2-6 | HIGH | The adjudication incorrectly says H3 makes “Ledger values are not cached” true |** `Adjudication:76` says “Under H3 it is true by construction,” referring to that sentence. H3 deliberately bakes ledger values into normally cached static files: `ADR:87–90,294–299`. Dating a cached observation does not make it uncached. **Smallest fix:** say H3 deliberately caches dated observations; H1 instead requires the specified loader-cache behavior.

Architecture `:257` correctly distinguishes completed round one from owed round two. **Smallest fix:** give E-327 that same distinction.

   117	**Seam 4: findings — H3 is a reasonable recommendation, incompletely priced.**
   118	
   119	H3 removes visitor-to-ledger traffic, the nonstandard WebSocket port dependency, reconnection work and browser cryptographic compatibility work. Revision 2 also avoids Gemini’s stronger unsupported claims that only these four accounts exist or that static generation prevents a lying build-time node.
   120	
   121	**R2-9 | MEDIUM | H3’s cost omits replacing the application’s runtime API dependency |** `ADR:152` prices a build-time ledger read, but today every route passes through `web/app/src/routes/__root.tsx:10`, which calls `assertOurApi`; `web/app/src/api.ts:66–80` requires `/api/meta`. Wallet and custody loaders still call the API (`wallet.tsx:6`, `custody.tsx:6`). A controlled 404 fetch made the root check refuse rendering. Prerendering alone does not remove those client navigation paths. **Smallest fix:** explicitly price a hosted static-data adapter or nonhydrated page target, and require direct-load and navigation checks with Elysia absent.
   122	
   123	**R2-10 | MEDIUM | The provenance rule leaves the owner-machine build execution boundary unpriced |** `ADR:98–105` controls checkout, dependencies, environment and maps; it does not constrain dependency/build code’s filesystem access. D-H4 places a deployment credential on that machine (`:241`). An output inventory cannot establish that build code never read another local file or credential. **Smallest fix:** name either an isolated build environment without private stores/deploy credentials or an explicit acceptance of that build-execution trust; keep deployment credentials out of dependency execution.
   124	
   125	Other outstanding H3 choices:
   126	
   127	- **Remote acceptance:** the existing reader requires the exact `WCONF_ACCEPT_REMOTE_LEDGER_RISK` sentence (`facility/src/loopback.ts:492–518`). With it unset, opening the proposed endpoint returned `REFUSED_NOT_LOOPBACK`. The hosted build script must explicitly name its endpoint and acceptance input under the environment allowlist.
   128	- **Reset rendering:** `ADR:262–267` specifies honest wording, but existing observer computation distinguishes missing MPToken, not absent AccountRoot (`web/api/observer.ts:126–145`). The build needs an explicit account-existence check and separate rendering for `actNotFound`, missing token and failed read.
   129	- **Rebuild cadence:** “whenever the owner wants fresher values” is an unresolved operational choice. The ADR does honestly limit reset detection to the next build; it does not promise immediate detection. Name the owner/cadence or explicitly accept indefinite historical snapshots.
   130	
   131	These costs do not overturn H3.

I temporarily restored the issuer claim in actual `index.tsx`: the exact single-line copy failed, but splitting after the comma passed. Restoring the wallet claim with a newline between “any” and “release” also passed. **Smallest fix:** normalize whitespace across logical text before matching and add controls for both actual JSX forms.

     5	## Question 1: Your own findings
     6	
     7	**Explicit verdict:** `ALL DISCHARGED; REGRADES HOLD`
     8	
     9	Each of the round-one findings B1-1 through B1-10 and the Question 6 argument is evaluated against [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md), the [adjudication](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md), and [ARCHITECTURE revision 10](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md#L407-L430):
    10	
    11	1. **B1-1 (Non-standard port 51233 firewall block):**
    12	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    13	   - *Regrade rationale:* Port 51233 egress blocking degrades accessibility on restricted corporate/guest networks; it does not cause a security breach or architectural failure. Under the project's severity definitions, HIGH is reserved for publishing false facts, leaking secrets, creating signing oracles, or undischarged findings. The regrade to MEDIUM holds.
    14	   - *Discharge:* In revision 2, D-H1 lists non-443 egress blocking as an explicit cost and records an unsearched 443 endpoint as a named gap ([ADR-0004:139-140](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L139-L140)). Under recommended Option H3, visitors never connect to Devnet, mooting the risk. **Discharged.**
    15	
    16	2. **B1-2 (URL query parameter leakage to Vercel access logs):**
    17	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld.
    18	   - *Discharge:* Under H3, pages are fixed (one per demo account) with no visitor input box ([ADR-0004:89](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L89)). Furthermore, carrying lookups in URLs is placed on the never-touch list ([ADR-0004:127](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L127)), listed as Rotation Trigger 4 ([ADR-0004:218](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L218)), and codified as an architecture falsifier ([ARCHITECTURE:429](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md#L429)). **Discharged.**
    19	
    20	3. **B1-3 (Vercel platform script and telemetry injection):**
    21	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld (with R1-10 and S-3).
    22	   - *Discharge:* D-H4 mandates disabling the Vercel toolbar for both preview and production environments ([ADR-0004:193-196](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L193-L196)). The gate check specifies delivered Content Security Policy headers (`default-src 'self'`, `script-src 'self'` plus hashes, `object-src 'none'`) in `.vercel/output/config.json` ([ADR-0004:228-232](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L228-L232)), blocking external injection from `vercel.live`. Step 9 adds a falsifier for served bytes or headers differing from gated output. **Discharged.**
    23	
    24	4. **B1-4 (Contradicting ADR-0003 by claiming gate enumerates egress):**
    25	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, HIGH.
    26	   - *Discharge:* Retracted in revision 2's status line ([ADR-0004:7-10](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L7-L10)). The body explicitly concedes that visitor requests are runtime egress no bundle contains, cites [ADR-0003](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0003-web-tier-trust-boundary.md), states that the gate enumerates uploaded files rather than egress ([ADR-0004:106-110](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L106-L110)), and repeats this boundary in the gate requirements and non-claims ([ADR-0004:236,242-243](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L236-L243)). **Discharged.**
    27	
    28	5. **B1-5 (Evasion of Step 8's loopback falsifier):**
    29	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    30	   - *Regrade rationale:* "Nothing to bind" was an evasive rhetorical phrasing rather than an architectural violation. The regrade to MEDIUM holds.
    31	   - *Discharge:* Revision 2 acknowledges the phrasing read as evasion ([ADR-0004:15](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L15)) and replaces it with the principled architectural position: the hosted page is public by design, step 8's loopback property does not carry over to it, and step 9's specific public static hosting falsifiers replace it ([ADR-0004:13-14](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L13-L14)). **Discharged.**
    32	
    33	6. **B1-6 (Privacy disclosure omitted Vercel):**
    34	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, HIGH.
    35	   - *Discharge:* D-H2 adds the mandatory second paragraph: *"Vercel, which hosts this page, sees your IP address and the pages you open."* ([ADR-0004:158](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L158)). Under H1, it discloses Ripple's node in addition to Vercel ([ADR-0004:159-160](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L159-L160)). **Discharged.**
    36	
    37	7. **B1-7 (Node failure disclosure / Devnet resets):**
    38	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld in part.
    39	   - *Partial uphold rationale:* Devnet testnet status, lack of economic value, periodic resets, and "lying or lagging" servers were incorporated into the disclosure. The adjudicator ruled that dropped network messages represent reader transport failures that must render as an explicit error UI ("named failure") rather than caveats in static data copy. This technical distinction is sound: transport failures should halt rendering, not caveat displayed data. The partial uphold holds.
    40	   - *Discharge:* D-H2 now explicitly states Devnet is a test network, tokens have no value, resets occur periodically, and servers may be lying or lagging ([ADR-0004:154-156](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L154-L156)). **Discharged.**
    41	
    42	8. **B1-8 (False claim that ledger values are not cached):**
    43	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    44	   - *Regrade rationale:* Stating a requirement as a fact without mechanism in an unbuilt proposal is a specification correction (MEDIUM), not an operational leak. The regrade holds.
    45	   - *Discharge:* Under H3, values are baked into static HTML, eliminating client caching bugs by construction ([ADR-0004:210](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L210)). Under H1, revision 2 specifies that route loader caching must be disabled with a regression test showing re-navigation re-reads the socket ([ADR-0004:212-214](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L212-L214)). **Discharged.**
    46	
    47	9. **B1-9 (Overclaiming single-reader reuse for browser WebSocket):**
    48	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    49	   - *Regrade rationale:* Correcting an overclaimed existing abstraction in an unbuilt design document is a costing/scoping correction (MEDIUM). The regrade holds.
    50	   - *Discharge:* Revision 2 acknowledges that [`facility/src/loopback.ts`](file:///facility/src/loopback.ts) cannot run in a browser and explicitly prices refactoring it to decouple transport from the `Snapshot` state machine as a required prerequisite for H1 ([ADR-0004:136,142](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L136-L142)). **Discharged.**
    51	
    52	10. **B1-10 (Unqualified claim of "no code of ours runs per request"):**
    53	    - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, LOW.
    54	    - *Discharge:* Changed in the specification to: *"No server-side code of ours runs per request. The deployment is static files."* ([ADR-0004:83](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L83)). **Discharged.**
    55	
    56	11. **Question 6 argument (Overturning H1 in favor of H3):**
    57	    - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld; D-H1 recommendation moved to H3.
    58	    - *Discharge:* Revision 2 adopts H3 as the primary recommendation in the status line ([ADR-0004:10](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L10)), the proposed architecture ([ADR-0004:76-93](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L76-L93)), D-H1 table ([ADR-0004:134](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L134)), and the rationale section ([ADR-0004:141-146](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L141-L146)). **Discharged.**
    59	
    60	*Question 1: no findings.*
    61	*What was tried:* Examined every occurrence of B1-1 through B1-10 in revision 2, checked whether old wording survived in [ARCHITECTURE revision 10](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md), verified all regrade rationales against the project severity rubric, and confirmed that every mechanism promised in the adjudication is present in the text.

    95	
    96	## Question 3: New sentences
    97	
    98	**Explicit verdict:** `FINDINGS IDENTIFIED (FALSE CITATION CLAIM, DISCREPANT COUNT, AND OVERSTATED BUILD READ COUNT)`
    99	
   100	### Findings for Question 3
   101	
   102	- `B2-1 | MEDIUM | Adjudication falsely claims all upheld findings are cited at their sites | "ADR-0004 revision 2, with every upheld finding cited at its site." (docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md:95) | Add missing citation tags in ADR-0004 revision 2: (REVIEW-058 B1-1) at line 139, (REVIEW-058 B1-2) at line 127, (REVIEW-058 B1-9) at line 137, and (REVIEW-058 S-1) at lines 126 and 232.`
   103	  *Context:* [REVIEW-058c line 95](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md#L95) asserts as a matter of completed fact that revision 2 cites every upheld finding at its site. While the substantive fixes are present in the text, four upheld findings—`B1-1` (port 51233 firewall risk), `B1-2` (URL query parameter prohibition), `B1-9` (loopback transport refactor prerequisite), and `S-1` (prohibition against asking for secrets)—appear zero times in [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md).
   104	
   105	- `B2-2 | LOW | Adjudication summary count of regraded findings does not match table | "Rejected: none. Six findings were regraded down, each for the reason given." (docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md:83) | Change sentence to: "Five findings were regraded down (R1-7, B1-1, B1-5, B1-8, B1-9) and one was narrowed in scope (B1-7), each for the reason given."`
   106	  *Context:* The table explicitly regrades down exactly five findings from HIGH to MEDIUM: R1-7, B1-1, B1-5, B1-8, and B1-9. Finding B1-7 was submitted as MEDIUM and ruled "Upheld in part" with its grade retained at MEDIUM. Stating "Six findings were regraded down" conflates a narrowing of scope with a severity regrade.
   107	
   108	- `B2-3 | MEDIUM | ADR-0004 rev 2 and Architecture §6 step 9 claim build reads Devnet "once per page" | "The build reads Devnet once per page, with the reader the local tier already uses, pinned to one validated index, and the page names that index and the date." (docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md:87-88; also ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:412) | Reword to: "The build opens one pinned snapshot of public Devnet state at a single validated index, reads the required entries for all demo pages, and bakes static pages each naming that index and date."`
   109	  *Context:* In this repository's terminology, a "read" is an individual ledger RPC query (`Snapshot.pinned` / `LoopbackLedger.call`). Rendering a single account observer page requires multiple distinct ledger reads: `account_info` (for sequence), `ledger_entry` for `MPToken` (for ciphertext and encryption keys), and `ledger_entry` for `MPTokenIssuance` (for issuance state and outstanding amount). A build cannot render an account view with "one read per page". Furthermore, if a build were to open a separate snapshot "once per page", each page could pin a different ledger index across cluster nodes, violating the core requirement of pinning to a single validated index.
   110	
   111	---
   112	
   113	## Question 4: The recommendation that moved
   114	
   115	**Explicit verdict:** `THE MOVE TO H3 IS SOUND; THE AUTHOR DID NOT OVER-CORRECT`
   116	
   117	### The strongest case that moving to H3 is wrong
   118	
   119	1. **Destroys the "demo" premise of the launch plan:** The launch plan (`PLAN-PUBLIC-LAUNCH-2026-09-22.md`, phase 2 step 1) explicitly specifies a hosted tier "so people can try it". Under H3, a visitor cannot enter an account, query an issuance, or interact with the page in any way. A static site displaying hardcoded historical numbers is a published report or article, not a demo application. It fails to prove empirically to a skeptical public that XLS-0096 works live on Devnet.
   120	2. **Guarantees immediate and permanent staleness:** Devnet ledgers close every 3–4 seconds, and Devnet is subject to periodic resets. Under H3, a static build is historical the moment it deploys. Within hours or days, the public Devnet node cluster prunes old ledgers from its history (as recorded in M3, complete ledger ranges have lower bounds around 5.1M). A visitor attempting to verify the displayed ledger index against Devnet using their own node or curl commands will receive `lgrNotFound`.
   121	3. **Excludes third-party testers:** Third-party institutional developers or validator operators wishing to inspect their own test issuances or accounts using this observer view cannot do so under H3.
   122	4. **H1's flaws were routine frontend engineering bugs:** The blockers identified for H1 in round one (`Buffer` polyfills, decoupling transport in `loopback.ts`, TanStack Router loader cache busting) were standard implementation tasks, not cryptographic flaws. Ripple already operates the Devnet node; routing test lookups to Devnet leaks no Mainnet secrets.
   123	
   124	### Why this case does NOT hold
   125	
   126	1. **Fidelity to P-3 (Dated observations over standing truth):** `CLAIMS.md` P-3 mandates treating ledger state as dated observations rather than standing truth. H3 embodies P-3 by construction: every value is visibly bound to a dated ledger index baked into static files. H1 presents a false veneer of "live truth" while masking pool flapping, cluster desynchronization (M3), and node lag.
   127	2. **Absolute egress boundary (Trigger 5):** Leg B proved in round one (B1-4) that build-time lexical gates cannot enumerate or enforce runtime network egress. Under H1, every visitor's IP and search queries are leaked to Ripple's node pool on non-standard port 51233. Under H3, runtime egress is strictly zero; visitors load static files exclusively from Vercel.
   128	3. **Eliminates the "Second Reader" divergence trap (E-141):** `CLAIMS.md` E-141 establishes that duplicate readers of a single ledger obligation inevitably drift. Round one uncovered that [`facility/src/loopback.ts`](file:///facility/src/loopback.ts) had already drifted from the Rust signer by omitting `entryNotFound` ledger validation and type checks (M10/R1-7). Writing a brand-new browser-compatible WebSocket reader for H1 introduces a third reader. H3 reuses the single existing, tested backend reader at build time.
   129	4. **Firewall realities (B1-1):** Port 51233 is routinely blocked by enterprise, bank, and institutional corporate firewalls. Deploying H1 would cause silent connection failures for the primary capital-markets audience.
   130	5. **Absence of a public Devnet confidential MPT ecosystem:** No arbitrary Devnet account holds confidential MPTs; creating and managing them requires custom ElGamal key generation and ZK proof construction that no consumer wallet supports. The only accounts on Devnet with active confidential balances are this project's four demo accounts (M4). H1's "lookup box" is an illusion that would return empty positions for any real visitor address.
   131	6. **Preserves the escape hatch:** Revision 2 does not delete H1; it prices its implementation honestly and notes: *"If 'so people can try it' means a visitor looking up an account of their own, H1 is the option, at the costs above."* ([ADR-0004:145-146](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L145-L146)).
   132	
   133	The move to H3 is architecturally sound and represents principled discipline rather than an over-correction.
   134	
   135	---
   136	
   137	## Question 5: The visitor-facing wording
   138	
   139	**Explicit verdict:** `FINDING IDENTIFIED (DISCLOSURE MISATTRIBUTES STALE DATA SOLELY TO NODE MALFEASANCE)`
   140	
   141	The revised two paragraphs in D-H2 ([ADR-0004:154-158](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L154-L158)):
   142	
   143	> *This page shows the XRPL Devnet, a test network. Its tokens have no value, and it is reset from time to time. Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown, and a lying or lagging server behind it would have made it wrong. Nothing on this page signs or submits. Do not use a value shown here to build a transaction.*
   144	>
   145	> *Vercel, which hosts this page, sees your IP address and the pages you open.*
   146	
   147	### Evaluation
   148	
   149	1. **Sentence accuracy:**
   150	   - Sentence 1 (*test network*), Sentence 2 (*no value, resets*), Sentence 4 (*nothing signs or submits*), Sentence 5 (*do not use to build transactions*), and Paragraph 2 (*Vercel sees IP and pages opened*) are accurate, honest, and directly address round-one findings B1-6, B1-7, and S-4.
   151	2. **Defect in Sentence 3 under H3:**
   152	   Sentence 3 states: *"Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown, and a lying or lagging server behind it would have made it wrong."*
   153	   Under the recommended H3 hosting shape, all values are baked into the page at build time. When a visitor views the hosted demo hours or days later, the displayed ledger state will not reflect current Devnet state. This divergence does not occur because Ripple's Devnet server was "lying or lagging"—the server may have been entirely honest and synchronized at build time. The discrepancy exists because **the page is an unrefreshed, static point-in-time snapshot**. Attributing wrongness exclusively to a "lying or lagging server" misinforms the visitor by directing them to suspect node malfeasance or cluster lag rather than ordinary build staleness.
   154	
   155	### Finding for Question 5
   156	
   157	- `B2-4 | MEDIUM | D-H2 wording misattributes stale data solely to a lying/lagging server | "Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown, and a lying or lagging server behind it would have made it wrong." (docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md:154-156) | Amend sentence to explicitly state that the page is a static snapshot: "Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown (and a lying or lagging server at that time would have made it wrong). This page is a static snapshot and does not update with subsequent ledger closes."`
   158	
   159	---

   161	## The stopping condition
   162	
   163	> **"no third round unless round two finds a fix that did not discharge its finding."**
   164	
   165	### Explicit Ruling on the Stopping Condition
   166	
   167	**The findings of this review DO NOT meet the stopping condition. A third round is NOT owed.**
   168	
   169	*Rationale:*
   170	- Every round-one finding from all three legs (Leg B's B1-1 to B1-10 and Q6, Leg A's R1-1 to R1-11, and Self's S-1 to S-8) was discharged in revision 2 and the shipped codebase. None of the round-one fixes failed to discharge its finding.
   171	- The four findings raised in this round (B2-1, B2-2, B2-3, B2-4) represent:
   172	  1. An editorial tracking omission in the adjudication's citation claim (B2-1);
   173	  2. A minor counting inconsistency in the adjudication summary (B2-2);
   174	  3. A technical overstatement regarding build-time read counts in newly drafted text (B2-3);
   175	  4. A completeness refinement in visitor-facing disclosure text tailored to H3 (B2-4).
   176	- None of these findings is an undischarged round-one defect. Under the project's explicit stopping rule, round two is complete and phase 2 may proceed to owner decision and implementation.
   177

   179	
   180	## Last: The single sentence most likely to be false
   181	
   182	> **`"ADR-0004 revision 2, with every upheld finding cited at its site."`**  
   183	> — [`docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md`, line 95](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-
   184	[`docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md`, line 95](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md#L95)
   185	
   186	**Why it is false:**
   187	This sentence asserts as an accomplished fact that [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md) cites every upheld finding at its corresponding site. 
   188	
   189	In reality, four upheld findings from round one—**`B1-1`** (port 51233 firewall risk), **`B1-2`** (banning account lookups in URL request lines), **`B1-9`** (decoupling transport in `loopback.ts` as a prerequisite for browser use), and **`S-1`** (prohibiting inputs asking for secrets)—are upheld in the adjudication table, yet appear **zero times** anywhere in the text of `ADR-0004` revision 2. While the substantive concepts were incorporated into the prose, the citation tags themselves were omitted, making the statement factually untrue.

+/**
+ * The text with every run of whitespace collapsed to one space, and for each
+ * character of the result the 0-based source line it came from.
+ */
+function flatten(text: string): { flat: string; lineAt: number[] } {
+  let flat = "";
+  const lineAt: number[] = [];
+  let line = 0;
+  let inSpace = false;
+  for (const ch of text) {
+    if (/\s/.test(ch)) {
+      if (!inSpace) {
+        flat += " ";
+        lineAt.push(line);
+        inSpace = true;
+      }
+      if (ch === "\n") line++;
+      continue;
+    }
+    inSpace = false;
+    flat += ch;
+    for (let k = 0; k < ch.length; k++) lineAt.push(line);
+  }
+  return { flat, lineAt };
+}
+

     2

     8	  second section, a wallet overclaim that survived in the capability matrix, and a
     9	  fix that dropped half of what its finding asked for (`REVIEW-058d`, `CLAIMS.md`
    10	  E-329). It also found the ledger reader still missing one of the Rust reader's

    16	  *"Trigger 5 fires only for what is put into the bundle on purpose, and the gate

    18	  to be false. It was. Visitors' requests are egress the bundle does not contain,
    19	  and the repository leg showed a build input outside the source reaching the
    20	  output. **D-H1's recommendation moves from H1 to H3.** Three defects round one
    21	  found were in shipped code, not in this document, and are fixed: the facility's
    22	  ledger reader accepted a "not found" answer from any ledger, and two pages of

    28	- **Blocks:** phase 2 steps 3 to 5, the build and the deployment.

    33	revision was too, and its rotation retracted its central claim (`CLAIMS.md` E-230).
    34	Revision 1 of this document repeated that shape, and its own rotation retracted

    37	---
    38	
    39	## What changes
    40	
    41	Step 8's tier is a process on the holder's own machine, bound to loopback, serving
    42	one operator. A hosted demo is a public URL that anyone can load. Two of this
    43	project's own conditions fire for any public deployment, which is why E-309 ruled
    44	it a trust-boundary change and not a deploy step:
    45	
    46	- Architecture §6 step 8 is *falsified if* *"the API binds to anything but
    47	  loopback"*.
    48	- `ADR-0003`'s trigger 5 fires when *"this tier makes any datum reachable by a
    49	  party who could not obtain that datum before the tier existed"*.
    50	
    51	**The hosted page is public by design, so step 8's loopback property does not
    52	carry over to it.** Step 9's own falsifiers replace it. ⚠ Revision 1 said here
    53	that step 8's falsifier *"has nothing to bind"*, which read as evading the
    54	property rather than replacing it (`REVIEW-058` B1-5).
    55	
    56	E-309 ruled the demo read-only: no hosted signer and no keys in the page. This
    57	document asks the question the launch plan names first: **what does the hosted
    58	tier serve that a public node does not, and what does it cause to be sent, and to
    59	whom?** The second half was missing from revision 1.
    60

    65	carried from a previous session.

    80	
    81	## The decision proposed: prerendered pages, no server of ours, no visitor egress but page loads
    82	
    83	```
    84	Owner's machine, at build time
    85	  └── the existing JSON-RPC reader ──► public Devnet endpoint   (one pinned snapshot for the whole build)
    86	        └── static pages, each naming that ledger index and the date
    87	
    88	Visitor's browser ── static files only ──► Vercel   (no functions, no middleware)
    89	```
    90	
    91	- **No server-side code of ours runs per request.** The deployment is static files.
    92	  ⚠ Revision 1 said *"no code of ours"*, and the page's own script runs in every
    93	  visitor's browser (`REVIEW-058` B1-10).
    94	- **Every ledger value is a dated reading.** The build opens one snapshot at one
    95	  validated index, with the reader the local tier already uses, and reads every
    96	  entry each page needs from it; every page names that index and the date. Nothing
    97	  refreshes it until the next build. ⚠ Revision 2 said the build read Devnet *"once
    98	  per page"*, which is neither one read nor one snapshot per page (`REVIEW-058`
    99	  B2-3).
   100	- **The visitor's browser sends nothing anywhere but Vercel.** The pages are fixed:
   101	  one per demo account, with no input, so no lookup travels in a URL.
   102	- **The capability matrix, the custody simulation and the wallet page are static.**
   103	  They are functions of this repository's own text (`web/api/capability.ts`,
   104	  `web/api/custody-sim.ts`).
   105	- **The issuer view is D-H3**, an owner decision.
   106	
   107	**What the hosted output may contain, by provenance.** Files built from a clean
   108	checkout of the public repository at a named commit, with its locked dependencies,
   109	with no environment variable admitted to the build except those named in the build
   110	script, and with no source maps. ⚠ Revision 1 described the output as *"the static
   111	output of the public repository's own source"* and treated that as a boundary. M11
   112	shows a build input outside the source reaching the output, so provenance is
   113	something the build has to enforce and the gate has to check, not a description
   114	(`REVIEW-058` R1-3).
   115	
   116	**The build machine is trusted, and that has to be a choice.** Installing
   117	dependencies and building runs third-party code with the build user's file access.
   118	An inventory of the output cannot show that the build never read a private store
   119	or a credential. Build where neither the private stores (`~/.wconf`, `/tmp/wconf`)
   120	nor the deployment token is reachable, and bring the token in only for the upload
   121	step; or accept the build machine's trust by name (`REVIEW-058` R2-10).
   122	
   123	**What the hosted tier causes to be sent, and to whom.** Each page load goes to
   124	Vercel, which sees the visitor's IP address and the page requested. Under H3 that
   125	is all. Under H1 the visitor's browser also connects to Ripple's Devnet node and
   126	sends every account it looks up. ⚠ **Revision 1 said trigger 5 fired only for what
   127	is put into the bundle, and that the gate could enumerate it. Both halves were
   128	false.** Visitors' requests are egress no bundle contains, and `ADR-0003` itself
   129	says its gate does not check what the tier emits or to whom. The gate can
   130	enumerate the uploaded files; it cannot enumerate egress (`REVIEW-058` B1-4, and
   131	the repository leg's closing finding).
   132	
   133	**Trigger 4, the decryption oracle.** No request reaches a decryption under any
   134	D-H3 option: A and B decrypt nothing at request time, and C adds a verifier. That
   135	is narrower than revision 1's *"cannot fire: there is no request handler"*. A
   136	static file server answers requests, and a pre-decrypted figure served to everyone
   137	is the result leaving, which is E-212's lesson. That is why D-H3 is a ruling.
   138	
   139	**Hosted exclusions.** The local tier's signer panel and its loader call
   140	(`web/app/src/routes/index.tsx:12-15,72-85`) are absent, not merely the signer
   141	page; ⚠ revision 1 called it a page. The `/api-doc` page describes an API that does
   142	not exist in the hosted build and is absent too. Two false sentences found on the
   143	local tier by round one are corrected at source: the front page promised the issuer
   144	view showed the holder *"decrypted"*, and the wallet page said three reasons
   145	~~*"cannot be closed by any release"*~~ after saying a wallet that held the key would
   146	close them (`REVIEW-058` R1-4, `CLAIMS.md` E-328). ⚠ Round two found the same
   147	permanence claim surviving in the capability matrix, which the wallet page renders
   148	(`web/api/capability.ts`, the browser-wallet row), and it is now scoped to the
   149	wallets examined (R2-2, E-329).
   150	
   151	## What the hosted surface may touch, and may never touch
   152	
   153	**May touch:** public Devnet ledger state, read from one pinned snapshot at build time, or
   154	under H1 from the visitor's browser over the one named WebSocket endpoint; static
   155	content built as the provenance rule above says; and, only if D-H3 rules it, one
   156	published snapshot file.
   157	
   158	**May never touch:** any server-side request handler; any socket client or the
   159	signer's socket path; any secret, seed or private key, the demo issuer key
   160	included; any decryption routine in the shipped output; any input that asks a
   161	visitor for a seed, a key or any secret (`REVIEW-058` S-1); any network but Devnet;
   162	any second ledger endpoint, as a fallback or otherwise; any analytics, toolbar or
   163	third-party script; and any account or issuance a visitor chooses, carried in a URL
   164	the host receives (B1-2).
   165	
   166	## The owner's decisions
   167	
   168	### D-H1. The hosting shape
   169	
   170	| option | cost | what it buys |
   171	|---|---|---|
   172	| **H3. Prerendered pages** *(recommended)* | a build step that opens one pinned snapshot with the existing reader, naming its endpoint and the remote-risk acceptance input in the build's allowlist; replacing the application's runtime dependency on the API, because every route today passes the root's `/api/meta` check and the wallet and custody loaders call the API, with data baked at build time and checked by loading each page directly and by navigating, with no API running (`REVIEW-058` R2-9); a check that each demo account exists, so a reset, a missing token and a failed read render differently; a rebuild cadence the owner names, or an accepted indefinite snapshot; a visitor cannot enter an account | no visitor request leaves Vercel; no second ledger reader; no WebSocket, reconnection or `Buffer` problem; every value is a dated reading, P-3's habit. ⚠ The build still reads one node, so D-H2's first paragraph still applies |
   173	| H1. Static site, the visitor's browser reads Devnet | a WebSocket transport under `Snapshot`, after `loopback.ts` is refactored to separate its transport (`REVIEW-058` B1-9); a browser-safe replacement for the harness's `Buffer` helpers; snapshot integrity across reconnects; a Content Security Policy that admits the node; no loader cache reused across navigation | a visitor can look up any Devnet account. ⚠ Their IP and every lookup go to Ripple's node. Port 51233 is not 443, and networks that block other ports will fail the page (B1-1); whether a Devnet WebSocket endpoint on 443 exists has **not been searched** |
   174	| H2. Vercel functions running the Elysia API against Devnet over JSON-RPC, with SSR | the API on the public internet: CORS for the deployed origin, rate limiting, caching, function logs holding visitors' IPs and queries, and billing exposure on the owner's account | the existing reader unchanged, and visitors' IPs hidden from Ripple's node. ⚠ Every route added later runs our code per request, which is trigger 4's shape |
   175	
   176	**Why H3.** Revision 1 recommended H1, and round one priced what H1 costs. Its
   177	reuse fails in a browser as written (M5); the reader it would reuse had a gap the
   178	Rust reader closed long ago (M10); it adds a second transport, reconnection, a CSP
   179	exception and a cache rule; and it sends every visitor's lookups to a second
   180	party. What it buys is a lookup box. The confidential positions this demo shows are
   181	the project's own four demo accounts, and most readers of the posts will have no
   182	Devnet account to look up. H3 shows the same views, as dated readings, with nothing to
   183	reconcile. ⚠ The argument for H3 is the Gemini leg's, upheld (`REVIEW-058` Q6).
   184	**If "so people can try it" means a visitor looking up an account of their own,
   185	H1 is the option, at the costs above.**
   186	
   187	### D-H2. The ledger-source risk acceptance for a hosted page
   188	
   189	B-15's named risk acceptance was written for a local operator who chose their
   190	node (E-110, extended to the facility demo by E-151). A hosted page needs its own,
   191	and accepting a risk is the owner's act, not this document's. The proposed wording,
   192	shown beside every ledger value:
   193	
   194	> *This page shows the XRPL Devnet, a test network. Its tokens have no value, and
   195	> it is reset from time to time. Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet
   196	> endpoint, at the ledger and date shown, and a lying or lagging server behind it
   197	> would have made it wrong. The page does not update: it shows Devnet as it was at
   198	> that ledger, not as it is now. Nothing on this page signs or submits. Do not use a
   199	> value shown here to build a transaction.*
   200	>
   201	> *Vercel, which hosts this page, sees your IP address and the pages you open.*
   202	
   203	Under H1 the second paragraph gains: *"Your browser also connects to the public
   204	Devnet node operated by Ripple, which sees your IP address and every account you
   205	look up."*
   206	
   207	⚠ Revision 1's wording said *"no amount can be published because of it"*, which is
   208	true of the page and not of a reader who copies a displayed key into their own
   209	tooling, where a lying node reaches F-23 through them. It named only Ripple as a
   210	party that sees visitors, and Vercel sees every one. It omitted that Devnet has no
   211	value and is reset (`REVIEW-058` B1-6, B1-7, S-4). ⚠ Revision 2's wording blamed any
   212	difference from today's ledger on a lying or lagging server, when under H3 the page
   213	is simply a fixed reading (B2-4). The public endpoint also keeps only recent
   214	history, about a million ledgers on 2026-10-18 (E-319), so a visitor can check the
   215	ledger shown against it for some weeks and not after.
   216	
   217	| option | what it means |
   218	|---|---|
   219	| **Accept, by name, with this wording beside every ledger value** *(recommended)* | the same shape as E-151: the risk is unmet by name, and the page says so where the value is |
   220	| Do not accept | no hosted demo with ledger values. ⚠ Revision 1 offered H3 here as the way to avoid the acceptance, and H3 reads one node too, at build time (`REVIEW-058` S-5) |
   221	
   222	Corroboration across independently operated Devnet nodes is not offered as an
   223	option. Every Devnet host this repository names is under `rippletest.net`
   224	(`git grep -ohiE` for `devnet` hostnames, 2026-10-19), so every endpoint it knows
   225	has one operator. Whether an independently operated public Devnet node exists has
   226	**not been searched** outside this repository. That is a named gap, not a
   227	negative.
   228	
   229	### D-H3. The issuer view: what, if anything, the hosted page says the issuer reads
   230	
   231	`ADR-0003`'s ruled position for the local tier is E-241: the route reports
   232	whether a balance is decryptable and never the figure, because *"the plaintext
   233	integer only ever demonstrated that THIS PROJECT holds the demo issuer key."* The
   234	launch plan's phase 2 text proposes the opposite for the hosted page: the
   235	snapshot *"is the point of the demo and becomes a deliberately published static
   236	artifact over throwaway Devnet accounts, with the disclosure beside it."* The two
   237	cannot both stand without a ruling.
   238	
   239	| option | cost | what it buys |
   240	|---|---|---|
   241	| **A. No figure** *(recommended)*. The page shows `IssuerEncryptedBalance` present and populated under the issuance's `IssuerEncryptionKey`, and says the issuer can always decrypt it (P-9) | none beyond the static page | E-241's position, carried unchanged to a public audience. The ciphertext and the key it is under are ledger readings a visitor can check. That the issuer can decrypt it is the specification's, cited as P-9, and no figure is asserted |
   242	| B. A published snapshot of the issuer's figures | run `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` locally and publish its results with the ledger index and, for each holder, the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` the figure was decrypted from. The tool does not record that ciphertext today (`tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts:220-262`), so B changes the one tool that holds the issuer key | the contrast the plan wants: the observer sees a ciphertext, the issuer sees a number. ⚠ Nothing a visitor can run shows the figures are true. Re-reading each ciphertext shows only that the snapshot is current. Comparing the figures' sum with `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` shows nothing about the key either: that total is public, and anyone can split it among holders without decrypting anything (M9). The repository leg built two incompatible allocations of the same total at ledger 6,244,148, and both passed |
   243	| C. B, plus a proof of correct decryption that the browser verifies | unpriced. XLS-0096's clawback proof is a proof that the mirror encrypts a stated amount (M9), and M7 shows its verifier in the WASM build. ⚠ Two constraints from round one. A proof built with a real clawback context could be used by any account XLS-0096 lets submit a clawback for the issuer, delegates included, so a published proof needs its own domain-separated context naming Devnet and the ledger hash (`REVIEW-058` R1-5). And the published WASM module also exports amount decryption, so shipping it contradicts the rule against decryption routines in the output; C needs a verifier-only artifact (R1-6) | the only option under which a visitor can check each figure, and so the only one that answers E-241 |
   244	
   245	**Why A.** E-241's reasoning holds with more force on a public page. A figure no
   246	visitor can check is a claim with no falsifier, published under this project's
   247	name. B's checks cannot change that, because both pass for a publisher who never
   248	held the key, and the repository leg built that forgery. C is the way to show the
   249	number, and it needs the two constraints above priced first. Launching with A costs
   250	nothing, and C can be added later without reopening the rest of this decision.
   251	
   252	⚠ **This recommendation departs from the launch plan**, whose phase 2 text calls
   253	the snapshot *"the point of the demo"*. A draft of revision 1 recommended B with
   254	the sum check as its falsifier. Preparing the self leg showed that the check passes
   255	for a forger, so the draft changed before review.
   256	
   257	### D-H4. What Vercel deploys, and from where
   258	
   259	Read on 2026-10-19 from Vercel's own pages: `vercel.com/docs/cli/deploy`, marked
   260	updated 2026-09-18; `vercel.com/docs/git/vercel-for-github`, marked updated
   261	2026-09-17; and `vercel.com/docs/vercel-toolbar/managing-toolbar`, marked updated
   262	2026-08-11.
   263	
   264	| option | cost | what it buys |
   265	|---|---|---|
   266	| **A. Build and gate locally, deploy the prebuilt output from the CLI** *(recommended)* | a Vercel token on the owner's machine, which can replace what the public page says: scope it to the one project, give it an expiry, and keep it beside D8's export folder. Each deployment is a deliberate act. `vercel build` writes `.vercel/output`, and `vercel deploy --prebuilt` uploads that directory | the gate runs on the exact bytes that ship, which is phase 1 step 7's lesson. No additional repository credential or write permission is granted. ⚠ Revision 1 said *"no third party gains any access"*; Vercel still receives every file and controls how it is delivered (`REVIEW-058` R1-10, S-6) |
   267	| B. Vercel's Git integration, production from `main` | Vercel builds from source, so the gate has to run inside Vercel's build and fail it. ⚠ The Vercel for GitHub app's documented repository permissions include write access to `Administration`, `Contents`, `Pull Requests` and `Web Hooks`. That is a third party with write access to the public repository, which D8's option C exists to restrict | deploys follow merges. The integration deploys every push by default, previews included. A pull request from a fork needs a team member's authorization before it deploys, and that protection can be switched off. A fork's preview is a page under the owner's project saying whatever the fork says, which is E-209's presentation-integrity shape |
   268	
   269	**Why A.** It keeps the gate on the shipped bytes and keeps the public
   270	repository's write access where D8 put it.
   271	
   272	**Under either option, the host can add to the page.** Vercel's toolbar appears on
   273	preview deployments and on any environment where it is enabled, and it loads
   274	script from `vercel.live`. Set it off for both environments in the project, serve a
   275	policy that would block it anyway, and compare the served bytes and headers with
   276	the gated output after every deployment. ⚠ The deployed bytes are Vercel's to
   277	deliver; the comparison detects a change, it does not prevent one (`REVIEW-058`
   278	S-3, B1-3, R1-10).
   279	
   280	**The domain.** Serve it from its own `vercel.app` name or a domain used for
   281	nothing else. A subdomain of a domain that also hosts the owner's writing would
   282	share its cookie scope. A `vercel.app` name freed by deleting the project can be
   283	claimed by someone else, and every post linking it would then point at them.
   284	
   285	## Devnet resets
   286	
   287	The demo accounts are throwaway. Under H3 a page is a reading at a named ledger
   288	and date, and stays one: when Devnet resets, the next build finds the accounts
   289	gone and the page must say so rather than drop them.
   290	
   291	> *This account does not exist on Devnet at ledger N. Devnet is reset from time to
   292	> time and the demo accounts are throwaway. No other source is substituted.*
   293	
   294	A failed read must never render as an empty account, which reads exactly like a
   295	true statement about the ledger. ⚠ **That was not true of the reader this design
   296	reuses**: `facility/src/loopback.ts` turned a "not found" answer from any ledger
   297	into an empty position, and the observer view would have rendered it. It now
   298	requires, on every pinned answer, positive or negative, a numeric `ledger_index`
   299	equal to the pinned index and `validated: true`; it tolerates only `entryNotFound`;
   300	and it checks the type of every entry. Those are the Rust reader's checks
   301	(`signer/src/ledger.rs`, `came_from` and `ledger_entry`). ⚠ Round one's fix stopped
   302	at the index of a negative answer and revision 2 called that parity; round two
   303	found `validated` unchecked on both paths and the issuance's type check untested
   304	(M10, `REVIEW-058` R1-7, R2-4, R2-5). Old transaction hashes are
   305	not a foundation either, because the public endpoint stopped serving them without
   306	a reset (E-319).
   307	
   308	## Abuse, availability and cost
   309	
   310	Under H3 and H1 no server-side code of ours runs per request, so there is nothing
   311	of ours to rate limit. ⚠ This said *"nothing of ours"* in revision 2, a copy of the
   312	sentence round one corrected two sections up (`REVIEW-058` R2-1). Static hosting still has costs: page loads and transfer count against
   313	the owner's Vercel plan, and a flood of them can exhaust a free plan's quota or
   314	bill a paid one. Name the plan, its limits, a spend cap if the plan offers one, and
   315	who acts when the page goes down (`REVIEW-058` R1-11). Under H1 the node's limits
   316	are Ripple's, and the page must render a refused, failed or rate-limited read as a
   317	named failure, never as an empty account. Under H2 all of it becomes ours.
   318	
   319	**Copies.** The bundle is public and anyone can host a copy that reads a node of
   320	their choosing. Nothing prevents that. The page names its canonical address and
   321	the repository, and that is the whole defence.
   322	
   323	## Caching
   324	
   325	Under H3 the pages are cached, dated observations, deliberately: each names its
   326	ledger index and date and none claims to be current. ⚠ `REVIEW-058c` said H3 made
   327	*"Ledger values are not cached"* true by construction, and it does the opposite
   328	(R2-6). ⚠ Revision 1 said *"Ledger values are not
   329	cached"* without a mechanism, and under H1 TanStack Router's loaders keep data
   330	across navigation by default (`REVIEW-058` B1-8). Under H1 the build must disable
   331	loader caching for ledger reads, and a test must show that returning to a view
   332	reads again.
   333	
   334	## Rotation triggers
   335	
   336	`ADR-0003`'s five triggers apply to the hosted build unchanged. Four more are
   337	specific to hosting:
   338	
   339	1. **Anything that runs code of ours per request**: a serverless function, edge
   340	   middleware, or on-demand rendering. That is H2, and it needs its own rotation.
   341	2. **Any datum in the shipped output that the provenance rule does not admit.**
   342	   D-H3's snapshot is the only exception that can be ruled, and only by name.
   343	3. **A second network endpoint**, a fallback, or any widening of the page's
   344	   permitted connection targets.
   345	4. **Any new party that receives a visitor's requests**, and any visitor input
   346	   that leaves the browser. Moving from H3 to H1 is one.
   347	
   348	## What phase 2 step 4's gate must check
   349	
   350	The local gate, `tools/check-web-readonly.ts`, asserts the local shape. The hosted
   351	build needs its own part, run over the **uploaded output directory and its routing
   352	configuration**, not the source:
   353	
   354	- an inventory of every file in the output, compared with what the provenance rule
   355	  admits; no source maps;
   356	- no server function, API route, serverless function or middleware, in the files
   357	  or in `.vercel/output/config.json`;
   358	- no socket client, no signer socket path, no decryption routine and no secret-
   359	  shaped value. ⚠ **`check-no-secrets` cannot do the last part today**: it selects
   360	  files through git (`tools/check-no-secrets.ts:665-683`) and has no directory
   361	  mode, and the repository leg showed an ignored `dist/` file going unscanned
   362	  (`REVIEW-058` R1-9). The hosted part needs a directory mode with controls that
   363	  prove ignored build files are read;
   364	- the Content Security Policy as delivered, from `.vercel/output/config.json`'s
   365	  headers, with `default-src 'self'`, `script-src 'self'` plus hashes for any
   366	  inline script the framework emits, `connect-src 'self'` (and the one Devnet
   367	  WebSocket under H1), `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'none'`
   368	  and `frame-ancestors 'none'`; a policy set only on the development server does
   369	  not configure the CDN (`REVIEW-058` R1-8);
   370	- no input asking for a seed, a key or a secret;
   371	- every view that renders a ledger value also renders the D-H2 wording, the
   372	  network check and the ledger index;
   373	- the third-party licence notices the bundle's dependencies require, shipped with
   374	  it (`REVIEW-058` S-2);
   375	- the public commit the output was built from, and the gate's verdict on that
   376	  output, rendered on the page, so a reader can tell what built it and whether it
   377	  passed (S-8). ⚠ Revision 2 kept the commit and dropped the verdict, which the
   378	  finding asked for (`REVIEW-058` R2-3).
   379	
   380	It is verified by breaking it, in both directions, with its controls run through
   381	the real detector, and it says in its own output what it does not check. Gates in
   382	this repository have false-passed before they worked, `ADR-0003`'s own among them
   383	(E-165, E-182, E-226). ⚠ **It cannot check egress**: it reads files, and a visitor's
   384	requests are not in them.
   385	
   386	## What this decision does not claim
   387	
   388	- **That the hosted page is secure.** P-5 governs. It is a static demonstration.
   389	- **That the values it shows are true.** TLS to Ripple's node authenticates the
   390	  channel, not the values (architecture §7, F-23). D-H2 is the acceptance of that.
   391	- **That visitors are private.** Vercel sees every page load. Under H1, Ripple's
   392	  node sees each visitor's IP and lookups.
   393	- **That a holder is private from the issuer** (P-9), that confidential transfers
   394	  hide who or when (P-4, P-11), or that a fresh balance is hidden when F-1 says
   395	  it is publicly known to be zero. `web/api/disclosure.test.ts` pins the local
   396	  tier's wording, and the hosted build must run the same tests.
   397	- **That the gate covers egress, or proves the served bytes stay the gated ones.**
   398	  It checks files before upload; the comparison after deployment detects a change
   399	  at the moment it runs.
   400	- **That Devnet evidence is permanent.** E-319.
   401	
   402	## Before this ADR can be accepted
   403	
   404	1. The owner's rulings on D-H1 to D-H4.
   405	2. ~~Round one of the rotation.~~ **Run 2026-10-19, `REVIEW-058`.** ~~Round two.~~
   406	   **Run the same day, and its stopping condition fired**: three of round one's fixes
   407	   had not discharged their findings (`REVIEW-058d`). Round three, on revision 3's
   408	   fixes, is owed and is the last the owner's review policy allows, so the rotation
   409	   closes after it whatever it finds.
     1	# REVIEW-058 round two, leg B — the fixes to round one, before any code
     2	
     3	---
     4	
     5	## Question 1: Your own findings
     6	
     7	**Explicit verdict:** `ALL DISCHARGED; REGRADES HOLD`
     8	
     9	Each of the round-one findings B1-1 through B1-10 and the Question 6 argument is evaluated against [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md), the [adjudication](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md), and [ARCHITECTURE revision 10](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md#L407-L430):
    10	
    11	1. **B1-1 (Non-standard port 51233 firewall block):**
    12	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    13	   - *Regrade rationale:* Port 51233 egress blocking degrades accessibility on restricted corporate/guest networks; it does not cause a security breach or architectural failure. Under the project's severity definitions, HIGH is reserved for publishing false facts, leaking secrets, creating signing oracles, or undischarged findings. The regrade to MEDIUM holds.
    14	   - *Discharge:* In revision 2, D-H1 lists non-443 egress blocking as an explicit cost and records an unsearched 443 endpoint as a named gap ([ADR-0004:139-140](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L139-L140)). Under recommended Option H3, visitors never connect to Devnet, mooting the risk. **Discharged.**
    15	
    16	2. **B1-2 (URL query parameter leakage to Vercel access logs):**
    17	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld.
    18	   - *Discharge:* Under H3, pages are fixed (one per demo account) with no visitor input box ([ADR-0004:89](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L89)). Furthermore, carrying lookups in URLs is placed on the never-touch list ([ADR-0004:127](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L127)), listed as Rotation Trigger 4 ([ADR-0004:218](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L218)), and codified as an architecture falsifier ([ARCHITECTURE:429](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md#L429)). **Discharged.**
    19	
    20	3. **B1-3 (Vercel platform script and telemetry injection):**
    21	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld (with R1-10 and S-3).
    22	   - *Discharge:* D-H4 mandates disabling the Vercel toolbar for both preview and production environments ([ADR-0004:193-196](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L193-L196)). The gate check specifies delivered Content Security Policy headers (`default-src 'self'`, `script-src 'self'` plus hashes, `object-src 'none'`) in `.vercel/output/config.json` ([ADR-0004:228-232](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L228-L232)), blocking external injection from `vercel.live`. Step 9 adds a falsifier for served bytes or headers differing from gated output. **Discharged.**
    23	
    24	4. **B1-4 (Contradicting ADR-0003 by claiming gate enumerates egress):**
    25	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, HIGH.
    26	   - *Discharge:* Retracted in revision 2's status line ([ADR-0004:7-10](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L7-L10)). The body explicitly concedes that visitor requests are runtime egress no bundle contains, cites [ADR-0003](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0003-web-tier-trust-boundary.md), states that the gate enumerates uploaded files rather than egress ([ADR-0004:106-110](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L106-L110)), and repeats this boundary in the gate requirements and non-claims ([ADR-0004:236,242-243](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L236-L243)). **Discharged.**
    27	
    28	5. **B1-5 (Evasion of Step 8's loopback falsifier):**
    29	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    30	   - *Regrade rationale:* "Nothing to bind" was an evasive rhetorical phrasing rather than an architectural violation. The regrade to MEDIUM holds.
    31	   - *Discharge:* Revision 2 acknowledges the phrasing read as evasion ([ADR-0004:15](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L15)) and replaces it with the principled architectural position: the hosted page is public by design, step 8's loopback property does not carry over to it, and step 9's specific public static hosting falsifiers replace it ([ADR-0004:13-14](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L13-L14)). **Discharged.**
    32	
    33	6. **B1-6 (Privacy disclosure omitted Vercel):**
    34	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, HIGH.
    35	   - *Discharge:* D-H2 adds the mandatory second paragraph: *"Vercel, which hosts this page, sees your IP address and the pages you open."* ([ADR-0004:158](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L158)). Under H1, it discloses Ripple's node in addition to Vercel ([ADR-0004:159-160](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L159-L160)). **Discharged.**
    36	
    37	7. **B1-7 (Node failure disclosure / Devnet resets):**
    38	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld in part.
    39	   - *Partial uphold rationale:* Devnet testnet status, lack of economic value, periodic resets, and "lying or lagging" servers were incorporated into the disclosure. The adjudicator ruled that dropped network messages represent reader transport failures that must render as an explicit error UI ("named failure") rather than caveats in static data copy. This technical distinction is sound: transport failures should halt rendering, not caveat displayed data. The partial uphold holds.
    40	   - *Discharge:* D-H2 now explicitly states Devnet is a test network, tokens have no value, resets occur periodically, and servers may be lying or lagging ([ADR-0004:154-156](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L154-L156)). **Discharged.**
    41	
    42	8. **B1-8 (False claim that ledger values are not cached):**
    43	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    44	   - *Regrade rationale:* Stating a requirement as a fact without mechanism in an unbuilt proposal is a specification correction (MEDIUM), not an operational leak. The regrade holds.
    45	   - *Discharge:* Under H3, values are baked into static HTML, eliminating client caching bugs by construction ([ADR-0004:210](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L210)). Under H1, revision 2 specifies that route loader caching must be disabled with a regression test showing re-navigation re-reads the socket ([ADR-0004:212-214](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L212-L214)). **Discharged.**
    46	
    47	9. **B1-9 (Overclaiming single-reader reuse for browser WebSocket):**
    48	   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
    49	   - *Regrade rationale:* Correcting an overclaimed existing abstraction in an unbuilt design document is a costing/scoping correction (MEDIUM). The regrade holds.
    50	   - *Discharge:* Revision 2 acknowledges that [`facility/src/loopback.ts`](file:///facility/src/loopback.ts) cannot run in a browser and explicitly prices refactoring it to decouple transport from the `Snapshot` state machine as a required prerequisite for H1 ([ADR-0004:136,142](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L136-L142)). **Discharged.**
    51	
    52	10. **B1-10 (Unqualified claim of "no code of ours runs per request"):**
    53	    - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, LOW.
    54	    - *Discharge:* Changed in the specification to: *"No server-side code of ours runs per request. The deployment is static files."* ([ADR-0004:83](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L83)). **Discharged.**
    55	
    56	11. **Question 6 argument (Overturning H1 in favor of H3):**
    57	    - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld; D-H1 recommendation moved to H3.
    58	    - *Discharge:* Revision 2 adopts H3 as the primary recommendation in the status line ([ADR-0004:10](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L10)), the proposed architecture ([ADR-0004:76-93](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L76-L93)), D-H1 table ([ADR-0004:134](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L134)), and the rationale section ([ADR-0004:141-146](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L141-L146)). **Discharged.**
    59	
    60	*Question 1: no findings.*
    61	*What was tried:* Examined every occurrence of B1-1 through B1-10 in revision 2, checked whether old wording survived in [ARCHITECTURE revision 10](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md), verified all regrade rationales against the project severity rubric, and confirmed that every mechanism promised in the adjudication is present in the text.

    65	## Question 2: The other legs' findings
    66	
    67	**Explicit verdict:** `ALL DISCHARGED AS PROMISED`
    68	
    69	Every finding from Leg A (R1-1 to R1-11) and the Self leg (S-1 to S-8) was checked against the [adjudication table](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md#L45-L79) and [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md):
    70	
    71	- **R1-1 (Harness `Buffer` dependency):** M5 updated ([ADR-0004:60-63](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L60-L63)) and H1 cost updated ([ADR-0004:136-137](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L136-L137)).
    72	- **R1-2 (M2 tested one origin):** M2 corrected to distinguish one browser page from two non-browser Bun clients ([ADR-0004:49-53](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L49-L53)).
    73	- **R1-3 (Provenance rule):** Rule stated (clean checkout, locked deps, named build vars, no sourcemaps) at lines 95-103; file inventory codified in gate at line 221.
    74	- **R1-4 (Local tier false sentences):** Corrected at source in `index.tsx:51` and `wallet.tsx:78`; [ADR-0004:115-122](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L115-L122) records them; clarifies signer is a panel and loader call, not a page.
