    95	
    96	## Question 3: New sentences
    97	
    98	**Explicit verdict:** `FINDINGS IDENTIFIED (FALSE CITATION CLAIM, DISCREPANT COUNT, AND OVERSTATED BUILD READ COUNT)`
    99	
   100	### Findings for Question 3
   101	
   102	- `B2-1 | MEDIUM | Adjudication falsely claims all upheld findings are cited at their sites | "ADR-0004 revision 2, with every upheld finding cited at its site." (docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md:95) | Add missing citation tags in ADR-0004 revision 2: (REVIEW-058 B1-1) at line 139, (REVIEW-058 B1-2) at line 127, (REVIEW-058 B1-9) at line 137, and (REVIEW-058 S-1) at lines 126 and 232.`
   103	  *Context:* [REVIEW-058c line 95](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md#L95) asserts as a matter of completed fact that revision 2 cites every upheld finding at its site. While the substantive fixes are present in the text, four upheld findings—`B1-1` (port 51233 firewall risk), `B1-2` (URL query parameter prohibition), `B1-9` (loopback transport refactor prerequisite), and `S-1` (prohibition against asking for secrets)—appear zero times in [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md).
   104	
   105	- `B2-2 | LOW | Adjudication summary count of regraded findings does not match table | "Rejected: none. Six findings were regraded down, each for the reason given." (docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md:83) | Change sentence to: "Five findings were regraded down (R1-7, B1-1, B1-5, B1-8, B1-9) and one was narrowed in scope (B1-7), each for the reason given."`
   106	  *Context:* The table explicitly regrades down exactly five findings from HIGH to MEDIUM: R1-7, B1-1, B1-5, B1-8, and B1-9. Finding B1-7 was submitted as MEDIUM and ruled "Upheld in part" with its grade retained at MEDIUM. Stating "Six findings were regraded down" conflates a narrowing of scope with a severity regrade.
   107	
   108	- `B2-3 | MEDIUM | ADR-0004 rev 2 and Architecture §6 step 9 claim build reads Devnet "once per page" | "The build reads Devnet once per page, with the reader the local tier already uses, pinned to one validated index, and the page names that index and the date." (docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md:87-88; also ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:412) | Reword to: "The build opens one pinned snapshot of public Devnet state at a single validated index, reads the required entries for all demo pages, and bakes static pages each naming that index and date."`
   109	  *Context:* In this repository's terminology, a "read" is an individual ledger RPC query (`Snapshot.pinned` / `LoopbackLedger.call`). Rendering a single account observer page requires multiple distinct ledger reads: `account_info` (for sequence), `ledger_entry` for `MPToken` (for ciphertext and encryption keys), and `ledger_entry` for `MPTokenIssuance` (for issuance state and outstanding amount). A build cannot render an account view with "one read per page". Furthermore, if a build were to open a separate snapshot "once per page", each page could pin a different ledger index across cluster nodes, violating the core requirement of pinning to a single validated index.
   110	
   111	---
   112	
   113	## Question 4: The recommendation that moved
   114	
   115	**Explicit verdict:** `THE MOVE TO H3 IS SOUND; THE AUTHOR DID NOT OVER-CORRECT`
   116	
   117	### The strongest case that moving to H3 is wrong
   118	
   119	1. **Destroys the "demo" premise of the launch plan:** The launch plan (`PLAN-PUBLIC-LAUNCH-2026-09-22.md`, phase 2 step 1) explicitly specifies a hosted tier "so people can try it". Under H3, a visitor cannot enter an account, query an issuance, or interact with the page in any way. A static site displaying hardcoded historical numbers is a published report or article, not a demo application. It fails to prove empirically to a skeptical public that XLS-0096 works live on Devnet.
   120	2. **Guarantees immediate and permanent staleness:** Devnet ledgers close every 3–4 seconds, and Devnet is subject to periodic resets. Under H3, a static build is historical the moment it deploys. Within hours or days, the public Devnet node cluster prunes old ledgers from its history (as recorded in M3, complete ledger ranges have lower bounds around 5.1M). A visitor attempting to verify the displayed ledger index against Devnet using their own node or curl commands will receive `lgrNotFound`.
   121	3. **Excludes third-party testers:** Third-party institutional developers or validator operators wishing to inspect their own test issuances or accounts using this observer view cannot do so under H3.
   122	4. **H1's flaws were routine frontend engineering bugs:** The blockers identified for H1 in round one (`Buffer` polyfills, decoupling transport in `loopback.ts`, TanStack Router loader cache busting) were standard implementation tasks, not cryptographic flaws. Ripple already operates the Devnet node; routing test lookups to Devnet leaks no Mainnet secrets.
   123	
   124	### Why this case does NOT hold
   125	
   126	1. **Fidelity to P-3 (Dated observations over standing truth):** `CLAIMS.md` P-3 mandates treating ledger state as dated observations rather than standing truth. H3 embodies P-3 by construction: every value is visibly bound to a dated ledger index baked into static files. H1 presents a false veneer of "live truth" while masking pool flapping, cluster desynchronization (M3), and node lag.
   127	2. **Absolute egress boundary (Trigger 5):** Leg B proved in round one (B1-4) that build-time lexical gates cannot enumerate or enforce runtime network egress. Under H1, every visitor's IP and search queries are leaked to Ripple's node pool on non-standard port 51233. Under H3, runtime egress is strictly zero; visitors load static files exclusively from Vercel.
   128	3. **Eliminates the "Second Reader" divergence trap (E-141):** `CLAIMS.md` E-141 establishes that duplicate readers of a single ledger obligation inevitably drift. Round one uncovered that [`facility/src/loopback.ts`](file:///facility/src/loopback.ts) had already drifted from the Rust signer by omitting `entryNotFound` ledger validation and type checks (M10/R1-7). Writing a brand-new browser-compatible WebSocket reader for H1 introduces a third reader. H3 reuses the single existing, tested backend reader at build time.
   129	4. **Firewall realities (B1-1):** Port 51233 is routinely blocked by enterprise, bank, and institutional corporate firewalls. Deploying H1 would cause silent connection failures for the primary capital-markets audience.
   130	5. **Absence of a public Devnet confidential MPT ecosystem:** No arbitrary Devnet account holds confidential MPTs; creating and managing them requires custom ElGamal key generation and ZK proof construction that no consumer wallet supports. The only accounts on Devnet with active confidential balances are this project's four demo accounts (M4). H1's "lookup box" is an illusion that would return empty positions for any real visitor address.
   131	6. **Preserves the escape hatch:** Revision 2 does not delete H1; it prices its implementation honestly and notes: *"If 'so people can try it' means a visitor looking up an account of their own, H1 is the option, at the costs above."* ([ADR-0004:145-146](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L145-L146)).
   132	
   133	The move to H3 is architecturally sound and represents principled discipline rather than an over-correction.
   134	
   135	---
   136	
   137	## Question 5: The visitor-facing wording
   138	
   139	**Explicit verdict:** `FINDING IDENTIFIED (DISCLOSURE MISATTRIBUTES STALE DATA SOLELY TO NODE MALFEASANCE)`
   140	
   141	The revised two paragraphs in D-H2 ([ADR-0004:154-158](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L154-L158)):
   142	
   143	> *This page shows the XRPL Devnet, a test network. Its tokens have no value, and it is reset from time to time. Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown, and a lying or lagging server behind it would have made it wrong. Nothing on this page signs or submits. Do not use a value shown here to build a transaction.*
   144	>
   145	> *Vercel, which hosts this page, sees your IP address and the pages you open.*
   146	
   147	### Evaluation
   148	
   149	1. **Sentence accuracy:**
   150	   - Sentence 1 (*test network*), Sentence 2 (*no value, resets*), Sentence 4 (*nothing signs or submits*), Sentence 5 (*do not use to build transactions*), and Paragraph 2 (*Vercel sees IP and pages opened*) are accurate, honest, and directly address round-one findings B1-6, B1-7, and S-4.
   151	2. **Defect in Sentence 3 under H3:**
   152	   Sentence 3 states: *"Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown, and a lying or lagging server behind it would have made it wrong."*
   153	   Under the recommended H3 hosting shape, all values are baked into the page at build time. When a visitor views the hosted demo hours or days later, the displayed ledger state will not reflect current Devnet state. This divergence does not occur because Ripple's Devnet server was "lying or lagging"—the server may have been entirely honest and synchronized at build time. The discrepancy exists because **the page is an unrefreshed, static point-in-time snapshot**. Attributing wrongness exclusively to a "lying or lagging server" misinforms the visitor by directing them to suspect node malfeasance or cluster lag rather than ordinary build staleness.
   154	
   155	### Finding for Question 5
   156	
   157	- `B2-4 | MEDIUM | D-H2 wording misattributes stale data solely to a lying/lagging server | "Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown, and a lying or lagging server behind it would have made it wrong." (docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md:154-156) | Amend sentence to explicitly state that the page is a static snapshot: "Each value was read from Ripple's public Devnet endpoint, at the ledger and date shown (and a lying or lagging server at that time would have made it wrong). This page is a static snapshot and does not update with subsequent ledger closes."`
   158

   181	
   182	> **`"ADR-0004 revision 2, with every upheld finding cited at its site."`**  
   183	> — [`docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md`, line 95](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-
   184	[`docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md`, line 95](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md#L95)
   185	
   186	**Why it is false:**
   187	This sentence asserts as an accomplished fact that [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md) cites every upheld finding at its corresponding site. 
   188	
   189	In reality, four upheld findings from round one—**`B1-1`** (port 51233 firewall risk), **`B1-2`** (banning account lookups in URL request lines), **`B1-9`** (decoupling transport in `loopback.ts` as a prerequisite for browser use), and **`S-1`** (prohibiting inputs asking for secrets)—are upheld in the adjudication table, yet appear **zero times** anywhere in the text of `ADR-0004` revision 2. While the substantive concepts were incorporated into the prose, the citation tags themselves were omitted, making the statement factually untrue.
     1	# REVIEW-058c — adjudication of round one: the hosted demo's trust boundary
     2	
     3	- **Date:** 2026-10-19

     5	  architecture §6 step 9, at the frozen commit `d764bcaa75add41613a67d9cbb48c48038475cb4`.
     6	- **Why it ran:** the launch plan's phase 2 step 2 requires a full adversarial
     7	  rotation before any code, because a hosted deployment is a trust-boundary change
     8	  (`CLAIMS.md` E-309).

    26	
    27	Both prompts told the legs that the ADR recommended D-H3's option B. Leg A's seam
    28	3 said so outright, and leg B's question 3 and question 6 assumed it. The frozen
    29	ADR recommends A. The prompts were drafted before the self leg found that B's sum
    30	check passes for a forger, the draft moved to A, and nobody re-read the prompts
    31	against the frozen commit before launching them. **Both legs read the subject,
    32	noticed, and reviewed what it says.** Leg A: *"the checked-in ADR recommends **D-H3
    33	A**, not B"*. Leg B: *"This is factually false against the text of `ADR-0004`"*.
    34	
    35	This is E-160's shape with the adjudicator writing the packet, as in
    36	`REVIEW-048`. It cost nothing this time
    37	because both legs had the subject whole. **A prompt is part of the subject's
    38	history, and it has to be re-read against the frozen commit, not against the
    39	draft it was written beside.**

    92	## What leg B's question 3 confirmed
    93	
    94	Asked about B with a false premise, leg B still ruled on the substance: B *"routes
    95	around"* E-241 and passes for a forger, C is unpriced, A is sound. Leg A built the
    96	forgery: two incompatible allocations of the public total at ledger 6,244,148, both
    97	passing B's checks. The self leg's pre-freeze finding is therefore confirmed twice,
    98	once by argument and once by construction.
    99	
   100	## What changed in the subject
   101	
   102	- `ADR-0004` revision 2, ~~with every upheld finding cited at its site~~. ⚠ STRUCK by `REVIEW-058d` (E-329): B1-1, B1-2, B1-9 and S-1 were not cited in revision 2; revision 3 cites them.
   103	- Architecture §6 step 9 rewritten for H3, with the wider falsifier; revision 10
   104	  amended the same day.
   105	- `facility/src/loopback.ts`: negative answers checked against the pinned ledger,
   106	  and entry types checked. `facility/tests/loopback.test.ts` adds three tests, and
   107	  both test files' fake nodes now answer as rippled 3.4.1 does.
   108	- `web/app/src/routes/index.tsx`, `web/app/src/routes/wallet.tsx` and `ADR-0003`
   109	  D2 corrected; `tools/check-disclosure-phrases.ts` carries the three phrases, keyed
   110	  to E-328, and was broken by restoring the old copy.
   111	
   112	## Round two
   113	
   114	**Owed**, because round one found defects in the subject and in shipped code, and
   115	the fixes have been seen by nobody. Its stopping condition was set before round one
   116	ran and is unchanged: **no third round unless round two finds a fix that did not
   117	discharge its finding.** The owner's Codex policy allows two more plan rounds for
   118	this key.

Corroboration across independently operated Devnet nodes is not offered as an

229
### D-H3. The issuer view: what, if anything, the hosted page says the issuer reads
230

231
`ADR-0003`'s ruled position for the local tier is E-241: the route reports
232
whether a balance is decryptable and never the figure, because *"the plaintext
233
integer only ever demonstrated that THIS PROJECT holds the demo issuer key."* The
234
launch plan's phase 2 text proposes the opposite for the hosted page: the
235
snapshot *"is the point of the demo and becomes a deliberately published static
236
artifact over throwaway Devnet accounts, with the disclosure beside it."* The two
237
cannot both stand without a ruling.
238

239
| option | cost | what it buys |
240
|---|---|---|
241
| **A. No figure** *(recommended)*. The page shows `IssuerEncryptedBalance` present and populated under the issuance's `IssuerEncryptionKey`, and says the issuer can always decrypt it (P-9) | none beyond the static page | E-241's position, carried unchanged to a public audience. The ciphertext and the key it is under are ledger readings a visitor can check. That the issuer can decrypt it is the specification's, cited as P-9, and no figure is asserted |
242
| B. A published snapshot of the issuer's figures | run `tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts` locally and publish its results with the ledger index and, for each holder, the `IssuerEncryptedBalance` the figure was decrypted from. The tool does not record that ciphertext today (`tools/issuer-view-snapshot.ts:220-262`), so B changes the one tool that holds the issuer key | the contrast the plan wants: the observer sees a ciphertext, the issuer sees a number. ⚠ Nothing a visitor can run shows the figures are true. Re-reading each ciphertext shows only that the snapshot is current. Comparing the figures' sum with `ConfidentialOutstandingAmount` shows nothing about the key either: that total is public, and anyone can split it among holders without decrypting anything (M9). The repository leg built two incompatible allocations of the same total at ledger 6,244,148, and both passed |
243
| C. B, plus a proof of correct decryption that the browser verifies | unpriced. XLS-0096's clawback proof is a proof that the mirror encrypts a stated amount (M9), and M7 shows its verifier in the WASM build. ⚠ Two constraints from round one. A proof built with a real clawback context could be used by any account XLS-0096 lets submit a clawback for the issuer, delegates included, so a published proof needs its own domain-separated context naming Devnet and the ledger hash (`REVIEW-058` R1-5). And the published WASM module also exports amount decryption, so shipping it contradicts the rule against decryption routines in the output; C needs a verifier-only artifact (R1-6) | the only option under which a visitor can check each figure, and so the only one that answers E-241 |
244

245
**Why A.** E-241's reasoning holds with more force on a public page. A figure no
246
visitor can check is a claim with no falsifier, published under this project's
247
name. B's checks cannot change that, because both pass for a publisher who never
248
held the key, and the repository leg built that forgery. C is the way to show the
249
number, and it needs the two constraints above priced first. Launching with A costs
250
nothing, and C can be added later without reopening the rest of this decision.
251

252
⚠ **This recommendation departs from the launch plan**, whose phase 2 text calls
253
the snapshot *"the point of the demo"*. A draft of revision 1 recommended B with
254
the sum check as its falsifier. Preparing the self leg showed that the check passes
255
for a forger, so the draft changed before review.
256

257
### D-H4. What Vercel deploys, and from where
258

259
Read on 2026-10-19 from Vercel's own pages: `vercel.com/docs/cli/deploy`, marked
260
updated 2026-09-18; `vercel.com/docs/git/vercel-for-github`, marked updated
261
2026-09-17; and `vercel.com/docs/vercel-toolbar/managing-toolbar`, marked updated
262
2026-08-11.
263

264
| option | cost | what it buys |
265
|---|---|---|
266
| **A. Build and gate locally, deploy the prebuilt output from the CLI** *(recommended)* | a Vercel token on the owner's machine, which can replace what the public page says: scope it to the one project, give it an expiry, and keep it beside D8's export folder. Each deployment is a deliberate act. `vercel build` writes `.vercel/output`, and `vercel deploy --prebuilt` uploads that directory | the gate runs on the exact bytes that ship, which is phase 1 step 7's lesson. No additional repository credential or write permission is granted. ⚠ Revision 1 said *"no third party gains any access"*; Vercel still receives every file and controls how it is delivered (`REVIEW-058` R1-10, S-6) |
267
| B. Vercel's Git integration, production from `main` | Vercel builds from source, so the gate has to run inside Vercel's build and fail it. ⚠ The Vercel for GitHub app's documented repository permissions include write access to `Administration`, `Contents`, `Pull Requests` and `Web Hooks`. That is a third party with write access to the public repository, which D8's option C exists to restrict | deploys follow merges. The integration deploys every push by default, previews included. A pull request from a fork needs a team member's authorization before it deploys, and that protection can be switched off. A fork's preview is a page under the owner's project saying whatever the fork says, which is E-209's presentation-integrity shape |
268

269
**Why A.** It keeps the gate on the shipped bytes and keeps the public
270
repository's write access where D8 put it.
271

272
**Under either option, the host can add to the page.** Vercel's toolbar appears on
273
preview deployments and on any environment where it is enabled, and it loads
274
script from `vercel.live`. Set it off for both environments in the project, serve a
275
policy that would block it anyway, and compare the served bytes and headers with
276
the gated output after every deployment. ⚠ The deployed bytes are Vercel's to
277
deliver; the comparison detects a change, it does not prevent one (`REVIEW-058`
278
S-3, B1-3, R1-10).
279

280
**The domain.** Serve it from its own `vercel.app` name or a domain used for
281
nothing else. A subdomain of a domain that also hosts the owner's writing would
282
share its cookie scope. A `vercel.app` name freed by deleting the project can be
283
claimed by someone else, and every post linking it would then point at them.
284

285
## Devnet resets
286

287
The demo accounts are throwaway. Under H3 a page is a reading at a named ledger
288
and date, and stays one: when Devnet resets, the next build finds the accounts
289
gone and the page must say so rather than drop them.
290

291
> *This account does not exist on Devnet at ledger N. Devnet is reset from time to
292
> time and the demo accounts are throwaway. No other source is substituted.*
293

294
A failed read must never render as an empty account, which reads exactly like a
295
true statement about the ledger. ⚠ **That was not true of the reader this design
296
reuses**: `facility/src/loopback.ts` turned a "not found" answer from any ledger
297
into an empty position, and the observer view would have rendered it. It now
298
requires, on every pinned answer, positive or negative, a numeric `ledger_index`
299
equal to the pinned index and `validated: true`; it tolerates only `entryNotFound`;
300
and it checks the type of every entry. Those are the Rust reader's checks
301
(`signer/src/ledger.rs`, `came_from` and `ledger_entry`). ⚠ Round one's fix stopped
302
at the index of a negative answer and revision 2 called that parity; round two
303
found `validated` unchecked on both paths and the issuance's type check untested
304
(M10, `REVIEW-058` R1-7, R2-4, R2-5). Old transaction hashes are
305
not a foundation either, because the public endpoint stopped serving them without
306
a reset (E-319).
307

308
## Abuse, availability and cost
309

310
Under H3 and H1 no server-side code of ours runs per request, so there is nothing
311
of ours to rate limit. ⚠ This said *"nothing of ours"* in revision 2, a copy of the
312
sentence round one corrected two sections up (`REVIEW-058` R2-1). Static hosting still has costs: page loads and transfer count against
313
the owner's Vercel plan, and a flood of them can exhaust a free plan's quota or
314
bill a paid one. Name the plan, its limits, a spend cap if the plan offers one, and
315
who acts when the page goes down (`REVIEW-058` R1-11). Under H1 the node's limits
316
are Ripple's, and the page must render a refused, failed or rate-limited read as a
317
named failure, never as an empty account. Under H2 all of it becomes ours.
318

319
**Copies.** The bundle is public and anyone can host a copy that reads a node of
320
their choosing. Nothing prevents that. The page names its canonical address and
321
the repository, and that is the whole defence.
322

323
## Caching
324

325
Under H3 the pages are cached, dated observations, deliberately: each names its
326
ledger index and date and none claims to be current. ⚠ `REVIEW-058c` said H3 made
327
*"Ledger values are not cached"* true by construction, and it does the opposite
328
(R2-6). ⚠ Revision 1 said *"Ledger values are not
329
cached"* without a mechanism, and under H1 TanStack Router's loaders keep data
330
across navigation by default (`REVIEW-058` B1-8). Under H1 the build must disable
331
loader caching for ledger reads, and a test must show that returning to a view
332
reads again.
333

334
## Rotation triggers
335

336
`ADR-0003`'s five triggers apply to the hosted build unchanged. Four more are
337
specific to hosting:
338

339
1. **Anything that runs code of ours per request**: a serverless function, edge
340
   middleware, or on-demand rendering. That is H2, and it needs its own rotation.
341
2. **Any datum in the shipped output that the provenance rule does not admit.**
342
   D-H3's snapshot is the only exception that can be ruled, and only by name.
343
3. **A second network endpoint**, a fallback, or any widening of the page's
344
   permitted connection targets.
345
4. **Any new party that receives a visitor's requests**, and any visitor input
346
   that leaves the browser. Moving from H3 to H1 is one.
347

348
## What phase 2 step 4's gate must check
349

350
The local gate, `tools/check-web-readonly.ts`, asserts the local shape. The hosted
351
build needs its own part, run over the **uploaded output directory and its routing
352
configuration**, not the source:
353

354
- an inventory of every file in the output, compared with what the provenance rule
355
  admits; no source maps;
356
- no server function, API route, serverless function or middleware, in the files
357
  or in `.vercel/output/config.json`;
358
- no socket client, no signer socket path, no decryption routine and no secret-
359
  shaped value. ⚠ **`check-no-secrets` cannot do the last part today**: it selects
360
  files through git (`tools/check-no-secrets.ts:665-683`) and has no directory
361
  mode, and the repository leg showed an ignored `dist/` file going unscanned
362
  (`REVIEW-058` R1-9). The hosted part needs a directory mode with controls that
363
  prove ignored build files are read;
364
- the Content Security Policy as delivered, from `.vercel/output/config.json`'s
365
  headers, with `default-src 'self'`, `script-src 'self'` plus hashes for any
366
  inline script the framework emits, `connect-src 'self'` (and the one Devnet
367
  WebSocket under H1), `object-src 'none'`, `base-uri 'none'`, `form-action 'none'`
368
  and `frame-ancestors 'none'`; a policy set only on the development server does
369
  not configure the CDN (`REVIEW-058` R1-8);
370
- no input asking for a seed, a key or a secret;
371
- every view that renders a ledger value also renders the D-H2 wording, the
372
  network check and the ledger index;
373
- the third-party licence notices the bundle's dependencies require, shipped with
374
  it (`REVIEW-058` S-2);
375
- the public commit the output was built from, and the gate's verdict on that
376
  output, rendered on the page, so a reader can tell what built it and whether it
377
  passed (S-8). ⚠ Revision 2 kept the commit and dropped the verdict, which the
378
  finding asked for (`REVIEW-058` R2-3).
379

380
It is verified by breaking it, in both directions, with its controls run through
381
the real detector, and it says in its own output what it does not check. Gates in
382
this repository have false-passed before they worked, `ADR-0003`'s own among them
383
(E-165, E-182, E-226). ⚠ **It cannot check egress**: it reads files, and a visitor's
384
requests are not in them.
385

386
## What this decision does not claim
387

388
- **That the hosted page is secure.** P-5 governs. It is a static demonstration.
389
- **That the values it shows are true.** TLS to Ripple's node authenticates the
390
  channel, not the values (architecture §7, F-23). D-H2 is the acceptance of that.
391
- **That visitors are private.** Vercel sees every page load. Under H1, Ripple's
392
  node sees each visitor's IP and lookups.
393
- **That a holder is private from the issuer** (P-9), that confidential transfers
394
  hide who or when (P-4, P-11), or that a fresh balance is hidden when F-1 says
395
  it is publicly known to be zero. `web/api/disclosure.test.ts` pins the local
396
  tier's wording, and the hosted build must run the same tests.
397
- **That the gate covers egress, or proves the served bytes stay the gated ones.**
398
  It checks files before upload; the comparison after deployment detects a change
399
  at the moment it runs.
400
- **That Devnet evidence is permanent.** E-319.
401

402

404
1. The owner's rulings on D-H1 to D-H4.

   **Run the same day, and its stopping condition fired**: three of round one's fixes
407
   had not discharged their findings (`REVIEW-058d`). Round three, on revision 3's
408
   fixes, is owed and is the last the owner's review policy allows, so the rotation
409
   closes after it whatever it finds.

## Question 1: Your own findings
6

7
**Explicit verdict:** `ALL DISCHARGED; REGRADES HOLD`
8

9
Each of the round-one findings B1-1 through B1-10 and the Question 6 argument is evaluated against [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md), the [adjudication](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md), and [ARCHITECTURE revision 10](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md#L407-L430):
10

11
1. **B1-1 (Non-standard port 51233 firewall block):**
12
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
13
   - *Regrade rationale:* Port 51233 egress blocking degrades accessibility on restricted corporate/guest networks; it does not cause a security breach or architectural failure. Under the project's severity definitions, HIGH is reserved for publishing false facts, leaking secrets, creating signing oracles, or undischarged findings. The regrade to MEDIUM holds.
14
   - *Discharge:* In revision 2, D-H1 lists non-443 egress blocking as an explicit cost and records an unsearched 443 endpoint as a named gap ([ADR-0004:139-140](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L139-L140)). Under recommended Option H3, visitors never connect to Devnet, mooting the risk. **Discharged.**
15

16
2. **B1-2 (URL query parameter leakage to Vercel access logs):**
17
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld.
18
   - *Discharge:* Under H3, pages are fixed (one per demo account) with no visitor input box ([ADR-0004:89](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L89)). Furthermore, carrying lookups in URLs is placed on the never-touch list ([ADR-0004:127](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L127)), listed as Rotation Trigger 4 ([ADR-0004:218](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L218)), and codified as an architecture falsifier ([ARCHITECTURE:429](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md#L429)). **Discharged.**
19

20
3. **B1-3 (Vercel platform script and telemetry injection):**
21
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld (with R1-10 and S-3).
22
   - *Discharge:* D-H4 mandates disabling the Vercel toolbar for both preview and production environments ([ADR-0004:193-196](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L193-L196)). The gate check specifies delivered Content Security Policy headers (`default-src 'self'`, `script-src 'self'` plus hashes, `object-src 'none'`) in `.vercel/output/config.json` ([ADR-0004:228-232](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L228-L232)), blocking external injection from `vercel.live`. Step 9 adds a falsifier for served bytes or headers differing from gated output. **Discharged.**
23

24
4. **B1-4 (Contradicting ADR-0003 by claiming gate enumerates egress):**
25
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, HIGH.
26
   - *Discharge:* Retracted in revision 2's status line ([ADR-0004:7-10](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L7-L10)). The body explicitly concedes that visitor requests are runtime egress no bundle contains, cites [ADR-0003](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0003-web-tier-trust-boundary.md), states that the gate enumerates uploaded files rather than egress ([ADR-0004:106-110](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L106-L110)), and repeats this boundary in the gate requirements and non-claims ([ADR-0004:236,242-243](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L236-L243)). **Discharged.**
27

28
5. **B1-5 (Evasion of Step 8's loopback falsifier):**
29
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
30
   - *Regrade rationale:* "Nothing to bind" was an evasive rhetorical phrasing rather than an architectural violation. The regrade to MEDIUM holds.
31
   - *Discharge:* Revision 2 acknowledges the phrasing read as evasion ([ADR-0004:15](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L15)) and replaces it with the principled architectural position: the hosted page is public by design, step 8's loopback property does not carry over to it, and step 9's specific public static hosting falsifiers replace it ([ADR-0004:13-14](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L13-L14)). **Discharged.**
32

33
6. **B1-6 (Privacy disclosure omitted Vercel):**
34
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, HIGH.
35
   - *Discharge:* D-H2 adds the mandatory second paragraph: *"Vercel, which hosts this page, sees your IP address and the pages you open."* ([ADR-0004:158](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L158)). Under H1, it discloses Ripple's node in addition to Vercel ([ADR-0004:159-160](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L159-L160)). **Discharged.**
36

37
7. **B1-7 (Node failure disclosure / Devnet resets):**
38
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld in part.
39
   - *Partial uphold rationale:* Devnet testnet status, lack of economic value, periodic resets, and "lying or lagging" servers were incorporated into the disclosure. The adjudicator ruled that dropped network messages represent reader transport failures that must render as an explicit error UI ("named failure") rather than caveats in static data copy. This technical distinction is sound: transport failures should halt rendering, not caveat displayed data. The partial uphold holds.
40
   - *Discharge:* D-H2 now explicitly states Devnet is a test network, tokens have no value, resets occur periodically, and servers may be lying or lagging ([ADR-0004:154-156](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L154-L156)). **Discharged.**
41

42
8. **B1-8 (False claim that ledger values are not cached):**
43
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
44
   - *Regrade rationale:* Stating a requirement as a fact without mechanism in an unbuilt proposal is a specification correction (MEDIUM), not an operational leak. The regrade holds.
45
   - *Discharge:* Under H3, values are baked into static HTML, eliminating client caching bugs by construction ([ADR-0004:210](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L210)). Under H1, revision 2 specifies that route loader caching must be disabled with a regression test showing re-navigation re-reads the socket ([ADR-0004:212-214](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L212-L214)). **Discharged.**
46

47
9. **B1-9 (Overclaiming single-reader reuse for browser WebSocket):**
48
   - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, regraded MEDIUM.
49
   - *Regrade rationale:* Correcting an overclaimed existing abstraction in an unbuilt design document is a costing/scoping correction (MEDIUM). The regrade holds.
50
   - *Discharge:* Revision 2 acknowledges that [`facility/src/loopback.ts`](file:///facility/src/loopback.ts) cannot run in a browser and explicitly prices refactoring it to decouple transport from the `Snapshot` state machine as a required prerequisite for H1 ([ADR-0004:136,142](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L136-L142)). **Discharged.**
51

52
10. **B1-10 (Unqualified claim of "no code of ours runs per request"):**
53
    - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld, LOW.
54
    - *Discharge:* Changed in the specification to: *"No server-side code of ours runs per request. The deployment is static files."* ([ADR-0004:83](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L83)). **Discharged.**
55

56
11. **Question 6 argument (Overturning H1 in favor of H3):**
57
    - *Adjudication ruling:* Upheld; D-H1 recommendation moved to H3.
58
    - *Discharge:* Revision 2 adopts H3 as the primary recommendation in the status line ([ADR-0004:10](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L10)), the proposed architecture ([ADR-0004:76-93](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L76-L93)), D-H1 table ([ADR-0004:134](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L134)), and the rationale section ([ADR-0004:141-146](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md#L141-L146)). **Discharged.**
59

60
*Question 1: no findings.*
61
*What was tried:* Examined every occurrence of B1-1 through B1-10 in revision 2, checked whether old wording survived in [ARCHITECTURE revision 10](file:///docs/architecture/ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md), verified all regrade rationales against the project severity rubric, and confirmed that every mechanism promised in the adjudication is present in the text.

## Question 2: The other legs' findings
66

67
**Explicit verdict:** `ALL DISCHARGED AS PROMISED`

## Question 3: New sentences
97

98
**Explicit verdict:** `FINDINGS IDENTIFIED (FALSE CITATION CLAIM, DISCREPANT COUNT, AND OVERSTATED BUILD READ COUNT)`
99

100
### Findings for Question 3
101

102
- `B2-1 | MEDIUM | Adjudication falsely claims all upheld findings are cited at their sites | "ADR-0004 revision 2, with every upheld finding cited at its site." (docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md:95) | Add missing citation tags in ADR-0004 revision 2: (REVIEW-058 B1-1) at line 139, (REVIEW-058 B1-2) at line 127, (REVIEW-058 B1-9) at line 137, and (REVIEW-058 S-1) at lines 126 and 232.`
103
  *Context:* [REVIEW-058c line 95](file:///docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md#L95) asserts as a matter of completed fact that revision 2 cites every upheld finding at its site. While the substantive fixes are present in the text, four upheld findings—`B1-1` (port 51233 firewall risk), `B1-2` (URL query parameter prohibition), `B1-9` (loopback transport refactor prerequisite), and `S-1` (prohibition against asking for secrets)—appear zero times in [ADR-0004 revision 2](file:///docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md).
104

105
- `B2-2 | LOW | Adjudication summary count of regraded findings does not match table | "Rejected: none. Six findings were regraded down, each for the reason given." (docs/reviews/REVIEW-058c-adjudication-round-one-2026-10-19.md:83) | Change sentence to: "Five findings were regraded down (R1-7, B1-1, B1-5, B1-8, B1-9) and one was narrowed in scope (B1-7), each for the reason given."`
106
  *Context:* The table explicitly regrades down exactly five findings from HIGH to MEDIUM: R1-7, B1-1, B1-5, B1-8, and B1-9. Finding B1-7 was submitted as MEDIUM and ruled "Upheld in part" with its grade retained at MEDIUM. Stating "Six findings were regraded down" conflates a narrowing of scope with a severity regrade.
107

108
- `B2-3 | MEDIUM | ADR-0004 rev 2 and Architecture §6 step 9 claim build reads Devnet "once per page" | "The build reads Devnet once per page, with the reader the local tier already uses, pinned to one validated index, and the page names that index and the date." (docs/decisions/ADR-0004-hosted-demo-trust-boundary.md:87-88; also ARCHITECTURE-2026-08-18.md:412) | Reword to: "The build opens one pinned snapshot of public Devnet state at a single validated index, reads the required entries for all demo pages, and bakes static pages each naming that index and date."`
109
  *Context:* In this repository's terminology, a "read" is an individual ledger RPC query (`Snapshot.pinned` / `LoopbackLedger.call`). Rendering a single account observer page requires multiple distinct ledger reads: `account_info` (for sequence), `ledger_entry` for `MPToken` (for ciphertext and encryption keys), and `ledger_entry` for `MPTokenIssuance` (for issuance state and outstanding amount). A build cannot render an account view with "one read per page". Furthermore, if a build were to open a separate snapshot "once per page", each page could pin a different ledger index across cluster nodes, violating the core requirement of pinning to a single validated index.
110

## Question 4: The recommendation that moved
114

115
**Explicit verdict:** `THE MOVE TO H3 IS SOUND; THE AUTHOR DID NOT OVER-CORRECT`
116

117
### The strongest case that moving to H3 is wrong
118

119
1. **Destroys the "demo" premise of the launch plan:** The launch plan (`PLAN-PUBLIC-LAUNCH-2026-09-22.md`, phase 2 step 1) explicitly specifies a hosted tier "so people can try it". Under H3, a visitor cannot enter an account, query an issuance, or interact with the page in any way. A static site displaying hardcoded historical numbers is a published report or article, not a demo application. It fails to prove empirically to a skeptical public that XLS-0096 works live on Devnet.
120
2. **Guarantees immediate and permanent staleness:** Devnet ledgers close every 3–4 seconds, and Devnet is subject to periodic resets. Under H3, a static build is historical the moment it deploys. Within hours or days, the public Devnet node cluster prunes old ledgers from its history (as recorded in M3, complete ledger ranges have lower bounds around 5.1M). A visitor attempting to verify the displayed ledger index against Devnet using their own node or curl commands will receive `lgrNotFound`.

   178	const EXEMPT_DIRS = ["docs/reviews/", "node_modules", "/.git/", "/target/", "/dist/"];
   179	/** ⚠ This file necessarily contains every phrase. See "What it does NOT say" 4. */
   180	const EXEMPT_FILES = ["tools/check-disclosure-phrases.ts"];
   181	/**
   182	 * ⚠⚠ **`.tsx` WAS MISSING, SO THIS GATE COULD NOT SEE THE RENDERED UI AT ALL —
   183	 * `REVIEW-037` F1, `CLAIMS.md` E-234.** The web tier's pages are `.tsx`, and a
   184	 * page is **the largest disclosure surface this project has built**: it is the
   185	 * one place a claim reaches a human who cannot read the source. The gate that
   186	 * exists to stop a retracted claim being asserted was blind to every one of
   187	 * them. ⚠ **`.jsx` is included for the same reason, though the tier has none.**
   188	 */
   189	const LIVE_EXT = [".md", ".rs", ".ts", ".tsx", ".jsx", ".py", ".toml", ".json"];
   190	/** Lines either side of a hit that are searched for a marker. */
   191	const WINDOW = 6;
   192	
   193	/**
   194	 * ⚠ **One of these MUST be in the window before any hit is forgiven** — E-238.
   195	 * A phrase's own markers say *which* retraction; these say *that* it is one.
   196	 */
   197	const RETRACTION_TOKENS = ["~~", "STRUCK", "RETRACTED", "SUPERSEDED", "no longer"];
   198	
   199	export interface Hit {
   200	  file: string;
   201	  line: number;
   202	  phrase: string;
   203	  why: string;
   204	}
   205	
   206	function liveFiles(dir = "."): string[] {
   207	  const out: string[] = [];
   208	  for (const name of readdirSync(dir)) {
   209	    const p = join(dir, name);
   210	    if (EXEMPT_DIRS.some((d) => (p + "/").includes(d.replace(/^\.?\//, "")))) continue;
   211	    let st;
   212	    try { st = statSync(p); } catch { continue; }
   213	    if (st.isDirectory()) { out.push(...liveFiles(p)); continue; }
   214	    if (!LIVE_EXT.some((e) => p.endsWith(e))) continue;
   215	    if (EXEMPT_FILES.some((f) => p.endsWith(f))) continue;
   216	    out.push(p);
   217	  }
   218	  return out;
   219	}
   220	
   221	/**
   222	 * The text with every run of whitespace collapsed to one space, and for each
   223	 * character of the result the 0-based source line it came from.
   224	 */
   225	function flatten(text: string): { flat: string; lineAt: number[] } {
   226	  let flat = "";
   227	  const lineAt: number[] = [];
   228	  let line = 0;
   229	  let inSpace = false;
   230	  for (const ch of text) {
   231	    if (/\s/.test(ch)) {
   232	      if (!inSpace) {
   233	        flat += " ";
   234	        lineAt.push(line);
   235	        inSpace = true;
   236	      }
   237	      if (ch === "\n") line++;
   238	      continue;
   239	    }
   240	    inSpace = false;
   241	    flat += ch;
   242	    for (let k = 0; k < ch.length; k++) lineAt.push(line);
   243	  }
   244	  return { flat, lineAt };
   245	}
   246	
   247	/**
   248	 * The REAL detector. Every control below calls THIS — a control that
   249	 * re-implements the check proves only that the re-implementation works
   250	 * (`CLAIMS.md` E-182).
   251	 */
   252	export function scan(extra?: { name: string; text: string }): Hit[] {
   253	  const bad: Hit[] = [];
   254	  const items: Array<[string, string]> = liveFiles().map((f) => [f, readFileSync(f, "utf8")]);
   255	  if (extra) items.push([extra.name, extra.text]);
   256	  for (const [file, text] of items) {
   257	    const lines = text.split("\n");
   258	    // ⚠⚠ **A COPY WRAPPED ACROSS TWO LINES PASSED — `REVIEW-058` R2-11,
   259	    // `CLAIMS.md` E-329.** This matched each phrase within one source line, so
   260	    // the front page's false sentence passed once a line break fell after its
   261	    // comma, and so did the wallet sentence split before "release". JSX and
   262	    // Markdown both wrap prose. The text is now flattened, every run of
   263	    // whitespace to one space, and each hit reports the line it starts on.
   264	    const { flat, lineAt } = flatten(text);
   265	    for (const r of RETRACTED) {
   266	      const needle = r.phrase.replace(/\s+/g, " ");
   267	      for (let at = flat.indexOf(needle); at >= 0; at = flat.indexOf(needle, at + 1)) {
   268	        const i = lineAt[at];
   269	        const window = lines.slice(Math.max(0, i - WINDOW), i + WINDOW + 1).join("\n");
   270	        // ⚠⚠ **A RETRACTION TOKEN IS REQUIRED, NOT MERELY A CITATION —
   271	        // `CLAIMS.md` E-238.** E-232 tightened the marker LISTS after a review
   272	        // id forgave the assertion it was added to catch; the REVIEW-037
   273	        // spot-check proved the MODE survived that fix, because a bare
   274	        // `E-224` — an ordinary cross-reference that appears for a hundred
   275	        // unrelated reasons — still satisfied `markers.some()`.
   276	        //
   277	        // ⚠ **A citation says "this was discussed". A STRIKE says "this is
   278	        // withdrawn".** Only the second may forgive a live assertion, so a hit
   279	        // is now forgiven only when the window carries BOTH a retraction token
   280	        // AND one of the phrase's own markers.
   281	        // ⚠ The phrase's own marker lists CONTAIN the retraction tokens, so a
   282	        // naive `markers.some()` let a lone `RETRACTED` satisfy BOTH halves and
   283	        // the requirement was toothless. **Caught by this gate's own new
   284	        // control, before the fix was believed.** Identity and strike must come
   285	        // from disjoint sets.
   286	        const struck = RETRACTION_TOKENS.some((m) => window.includes(m));
   287	        const cited = r.markers
   288	          .filter((m) => !RETRACTION_TOKENS.includes(m))
   289	          .some((m) => window.includes(m));
   290	        if (!(struck && cited)) {
   291	          bad.push({ file, line: i + 1, phrase: r.phrase, why: r.why });
   292	        }
   293	      }
   294	    }
   295	  }
   296	  return bad;
   297	}
   298	
   299	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   300	
   301	if (import.meta.main) {
   302	  let failures = 0;
   303	  let controls = 0;
   304	  const control = (label: string, ok: boolean) => {
   305	    controls++;
   306	    console.log(`  CONTROL ${ok ? "✓" : "✗"} ${label}`);
   307	    if (!ok) failures++;
   308	  };
   309	
   310	  console.log("check-disclosure-phrases — no live file may assert a retracted disclosure");
   311	  console.log("  (docs/reviews/ is exempt: a packet is the record of what a leg was shown)\n");
   312	
   313	  // ⚠ A gate over an EMPTY set passes trivially and says nothing. Refuse first.
   314	  if (RETRACTED.length === 0) {
   315	    console.log("FAIL: the retracted-phrase list is empty — this gate would assert nothing");
   316	    process.exit(1);
   317	  }
   318	  console.log(`Scanning ${liveFiles().length} live files for ${RETRACTED.length} retracted phrases\n`);
   319	
   320	  const hits = scan();
   321	  if (hits.length) {
   322	    for (const h of hits) {
   323	      console.log(`  ⚠ ASSERTED: ${h.file}:${h.line} — "${h.phrase}"`);
   324	      console.log(`             ${h.why}`);
   325	    }
   326	    failures += hits.length;
   327	  } else {
   328	    console.log("  ✓ no live file asserts a retracted disclosure");
   329	  }
   330	
   331	  console.log("\nControls — each runs through the SAME detector the real check calls");
   332	  const p = RETRACTED[0];
   333	  control(
   334	    "an INJECTED retracted phrase with no correction marker IS caught",
   335	    scan({ name: "<injected>", text: `prose\n${p.phrase}\nmore prose` }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   336	  );
   337	  control(
   338	    "the SAME phrase with a retraction TOKEN and its marker is NOT caught",
   339	    !scan({ name: "<injected>", text: `⚠ RETRACTED, ${p.markers[0]}:\n~~${p.phrase}~~\n` }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   340	  );
   341	  // ⚠⚠ E-238. A citation is not a retraction. This is the mode E-232's fix left
   342	  // open, demonstrated by the REVIEW-037 spot-check with an unrelated `E-224`.
   343	  control(
   344	    "a BARE CITATION with no retraction token does NOT forgive the hit",
   345	    scan({ name: "<injected>", text: `⚠ see ${p.markers[0]} for background\n${p.phrase}\n` }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   346	  );
   347	  control(
   348	    "a retraction TOKEN alone, without the phrase's own marker, does NOT forgive",
   349	    scan({ name: "<injected>", text: `⚠ RETRACTED elsewhere\n${p.phrase}\n` }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   350	  );
   351	  control(
   352	    "a marker OUTSIDE the window does NOT forgive the hit",
   353	    scan({
   354	      name: "<injected>",
   355	      text: `${p.markers[0]}\n${"filler\n".repeat(WINDOW + 2)}${p.phrase}\n`,
   356	    }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   357	  );
   358	  control(
   359	    "clean prose is NOT caught",
   360	    scan({ name: "<injected>", text: "nothing retracted here at all\n" }).length === hits.length,
   361	  );
   362	  // ⚠ REVIEW-058 R2-11 and R2-12 (E-329): wrapped copies, and a true sentence
   363	  // that shares words with a retracted one.
   364	  const wrapped = p.phrase.replace(/ /, "\n");
   365	  control(
   366	    "a copy WRAPPED across two lines IS caught",
   367	    wrapped !== p.phrase &&
   368	      scan({ name: "<injected>", text: `prose\n${wrapped}\nmore prose` }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   369	  );
   370	  control(
   371	    "a copy wrapped and INDENTED, as JSX wraps it, IS caught",
   372	    scan({
   373	      name: "<injected>",
   374	      text: "          The three reasons above cannot be\n          closed by any release.\n",
   375	    }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   376	  );
   377	  control(
   378	    "a TRUE sentence that shares the retracted words is NOT caught",
   379	    !scan({
   380	      name: "<injected>",
   381	      text: "The protocol-mandated issuer mirror cannot be closed by any wallet update or any release.\n",
   382	    }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   383	  );
   384	  control(
   385	    "EVERY phrase in the list is detectable, not just the first",
   386	    RETRACTED.every((r) =>
   387	      scan({ name: "<injected>", text: r.phrase }).some((h) => h.file === "<injected>"),
   388	    ),
   389	  );
   390	
   391	  console.log("\n" + "-".repeat(74));
   392	  if (failures) {
   393	    console.log(`FAIL: ${hits.length} asserted retraction(s), ${failures - hits.length} control failure(s).`);
   394	    console.log("⚠ A retracted claim is standing in a live file. Grep the PHRASE, not the line.");
   395	    process.exit(1);
   396	  }
   397	  console.log(
   398	    `PASS: no live file asserts any of ${RETRACTED.length} retracted disclosures — under ${controls} controls,\n` +
   399	      "      each running through the same detector the real check calls.",
   400	  );
   401	  console.log("⚠ LEXICAL, over a NAMED SET. It catches COPIES, not paraphrases, and it");
   402	  console.log("  cannot tell whether a claim is true. See the header for what it does not say.");
   403	}

1.3.14
     1	/**
     2	 * loopback.ts — the facility's ONLY ledger read path.
     3	 *
     4	 * # Why this module exists, and why it refuses rather than warns
     5	 *
     6	 * `CLAIMS.md` **B-15**'s step-7 gate was ruled by the project owner on
     7	 * 2026-08-25: **the facility's ledger reads go to loopback.** Not "should"; the
     8	 * ruling is what opened architecture §6 step 7 at all, and `REVIEW-019` is
     9	 * explicit that *"we ran a local node once" is not the property* — the property
    10	 * is that **every** read the facility makes goes to a node the operator runs.
    11	 *
    12	 * What it is defending against is `CLAIMS.md` **F-23**, and F-23 is
    13	 * **executed**, not reasoned. A `ConfidentialMPTSend` encrypts the amount to
    14	 * the destination's `HolderEncryptionKey` and the issuer's
    15	 * `IssuerEncryptionKey`, **both read from the ledger**. A substituted key fails
    16	 * the proof with `tecBAD_PROOF` — and a `tec` is *applied to the ledger* with
    17	 * every ciphertext in it. On 2026-08-19 that was run on Devnet and the amount
    18	 * **42 was recovered from public ledger data by the party who supplied the
    19	 * wrong key**, while the intended destination could not decrypt it.
    20	 *
    21	 * So a lying ledger source does not cost a transaction. It costs the
    22	 * confidentiality of the amount, permanently, in public. **TLS does not address
    23	 * it** — TLS authenticates the channel to a node the operator chose, and a
    24	 * hostile node lies just as well over TLS.
    25	 *
    26	 * # ⚠ What this module does NOT buy
    27	 *
    28	 * Nothing about the same-host adversary the issue #1 ruling put in scope. That
    29	 * adversary can point this at a different endpoint, or edit the local node's
    30	 * answers. The trust boundary is unchanged and `CLAIMS.md` P-5 still forbids
    31	 * calling any of this secure.
    32	 *
    33	 * # Stricter than the signer, deliberately
    34	 *
    35	 * `signer/src/ledger.rs` accepts the *name* `localhost` as loopback. This
    36	 * accepts only an **address**, never a name, because a name is resolved by
    37	 * something this process does not control and there is no cost to requiring
    38	 * the address. `REVIEW-016` found the hosts `127.attacker.example` and
    39	 * `127.0.0.1@attacker.example` walking through the Rust check that exists
    40	 * *because of* F-23. Those two shapes are negative test inputs in
    41	 * `facility/tests/loopback.test.ts` — written there rather than quoted here, so
    42	 * that they are executed rather than described.
    43	 *
    44	 * ⚠ **"Address" means what `URL` canonicalises to, and that is wider than a
    45	 * dotted quad.** WHATWG `URL` folds `2130706433`, `0177.0.0.1`, `0x7f.1`,
    46	 * `127.1`, a trailing dot and even Unicode digit forms all to `127.0.0.1`
    47	 * before this check ever sees them, so **all of those are ACCEPTED** — and
    48	 * correctly, because every one of them genuinely is loopback. An earlier
    49	 * version of this comment said *"only a dotted-quad literal"* and the session's
    50	 * own review leg then asserted those forms were **refused**, from reasoning
    51	 * rather than from running them. `REVIEW-022`'s Codex leg ran them.
    52	 * `facility/tests/loopback.test.ts` now pins them as accepted, so that a later
    53	 * session cannot "tighten" the check into rejecting real loopback addresses.
    54	 *
    55	 * # ⚠ Redirects are refused, because a redirect is the whole check undone
    56	 *
    57	 * `fetch` follows redirects by default. A loopback endpoint answering `307`
    58	 * could forward the POST — and every one after it — to a remote host, and
    59	 * `LoopbackLedger.open`'s check would have passed on the original URL.
    60	 * `signer/src/ledger.rs` sets `max_redirects(0)` for exactly this reason and
    61	 * this file did not until `REVIEW-022`'s Codex leg found it.
    62	 */
    63	
    64	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    65	// The escape hatch, and everything about it is deliberate.
    66	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    67	
    68	/**
    69	 * The exact string `WCONF_ACCEPT_REMOTE_LEDGER_RISK` must hold before this
    70	 * module will read a ledger anywhere but loopback.
    71	 *
    72	 * ⚠ **It is a sentence, not a `1`, and that is the point.** A flag that can be
    73	 * set by muscle memory is a flag that gets set by muscle memory. Somebody has
    74	 * to type this, and typing it is the acknowledgement.
    75	 *
    76	 * ⚠ **This does not make a remote endpoint safe. It makes it RECORDED.**
    77	 * `CLAIMS.md` F-23 is executed: on 2026-08-19 an amount was recovered from
    78	 * public ledger data by the party who supplied a wrong key. Nothing here
    79	 * changes that; what changes is that a run which accepted the risk says so, in
    80	 * its output and in its own run record.
    81	 */
    82	export const RISK_ACCEPTANCE_PHRASE =
    83	  "I accept CLAIMS.md F-23: a lying ledger source publishes the amount";
    84	
    85	/**
    86	 * Printed to stderr, in full, on every run that uses the hatch.
    87	 *
    88	 * ⚠ **A constant with an asserted wording**, because `CLAIMS.md` E-126 records
    89	 * that the one disclosure surface which was not a constant is where a false
    90	 * sentence lived through a whole round of review — and `REVIEW-022` then found
    91	 * this file's sibling citing a wording test that did not exist. This one has
    92	 * `facility/tests/risk-acceptance.test.ts`.
    93	 */
    94	export const REMOTE_LEDGER_DISCLOSURE = [
    95	  "⚠ THIS RUN IS NOT UNDER B-15's STEP-7 CONDITIONS.",
    96	  "",
    97	  "  The facility's ledger source is REMOTE and is trusted for amount",

   100	  "  amount encrypted under the liar's key, permanently, in public. TLS does not",
   101	  "  address it — it authenticates the channel to a node someone chose.",

   104	  "  extend REVIEW-019's named risk acceptance to this demo. That amendment, like",
   105	  "  the ruling it amends, has had NO adversarial review.",
   106	  "",
   107	  "  What a run under this hatch CAN establish: that the transactions serialise,",
   108	  "  that rippled accepts them, that the balances decrypt to what the book",
   109	  "  predicts, and what a full-ledger observer sees.",
   110	  "",

   112	  "  did, while run-facility.ts's own header said it did not — REVIEW-023 found the",
   113	  "  pair. The driver checks the outstanding-amount delta on the float conversion",
   114	  "  and reconciles decrypted balances; there are no per-movement assertions over",
   115	  "  the public amount or the balance version. CLAIMS.md B-18.",
